*Troparion 1:* O Sovereign Lady of the world, Theotokos! Raise me up who am evilly submerged in the depth of evil perdition by carnal lusts and attachment to the things of this life, thou who alone art merciful; for I have no hope of salvation at all; for I am wholly in despair, O Pure One.[^1002]

*Troparion 2:* Thou art the salvation of all men, who didst give birth unto God in an ineffable manner; thou art the savior of the faithful, O Theotokos, the guide of the blind, and the restoration of the fallen. Therefore, praising thee, we call unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him supreme unto all ages.[^1003]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having acquired thee as a most firm wall, we have placed the hope of our salvation in thee, O Mother of God! But be thou a harbor unto thy servants, and an insurmountable rampart; and direct us unceasingly to cry aloud: Let every creature bless the Lord, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages.[^1004]

*Both now...*
*Stavrotheotokion:* Torn in maternal inward parts and filled with many tears, she that purely bare Thee, beholding Thee upon the Cross, cried aloud with disconsolate groanings: Having escaped pangs at Thy birth, O my Son, I am now seized with pain, seeing Thy countenance dishonored.[^1005]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Ineffable is the mystery of the Virgin: for she was shown forth as heaven, and the Cherubic throne, and the light-bearing bridal chamber of Christ God the Almighty. Her do we piously magnify as the Theotokos.[^1006]

*Troparion 1:* With the passions of dishonor have I, wretched one, defiled my soul, and with soul-corrupting pleasures have I stained all my flesh; but as thou art pure and undefiled, cleanse me by the multitude of thy mercy.[^1007]

*Troparion 2:* I have acquired no refuge beside thee, O Pure One; I know no other firm protectress and shelter upon earth, O Sovereign Lady; therefore I have fervently run unto thee, asking to receive through thee deliverance from transgressions.[^1008]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* O all-lauded one, look mercifully from on high upon thy servants now, preserving them in pious faith, and by thy prayers delivering us from every tribulation, who honor thee as the true, pure Theotokos.[^1009]

*Both now...*
*Stavrotheotokion:* She who knew seedless birth, when she beheld Thee, O Lover of mankind, suspended upon the wood of the Cross, cried aloud: O my Son and God almighty, Who desirest to save men, how hast Thou now accepted crucifixion?[^1010]

*Then: It is truly meet: Trisagion. After Our Father: troparia and the rest, and the Dismissal.*[^1011]

*On Friday Morning, At Matins.*
*After the 1st Stichologia, Sessional Hymns of the Cross, Tone 1:*
When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, the tyranny perished and the power of the enemy was trampled down; for neither an Angel nor a man, but Thou Thyself, O Lord, hast saved us. Glory to Thee.[^1012]

*Verse: Exalt ye the Lord our God, and worship at His footstool, for He is holy [LXX Ps. 98:5].*
We worship the tree of Thy Cross, O Lover of mankind; for thereon wast Thou nailed, O Life of all; Thou didst open paradise, O Savior, unto the thief who came unto Thee with faith, and didst vouchsafe the delight of paradise unto him who confessed Thee: Remember me, O Lord! Receive as him also us who cry unto Thee: We all have sinned; in Thy tender-heartedness despise us not.[^1013]

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd suspended dead upon the tree, the undefiled Ewe-lamb, weeping, uttered, crying out as a Mother: How shall I endure Thine ineffable descent, O my Son, and Thy voluntary passions, O all-good God?[^1014]

*After the 2nd Stichologia, Sessional Hymns of the Cross, Tone 1:*
The weapon of the Cross was shown forth of old in battles unto the pious emperor Constantine as an invincible victory over enemies, by reason of faith; at this do the adverse powers tremble; this hath become salvation unto the faithful, and the boast of Paul.[^1015]

*Verse: But God is our King before the ages, He hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth [LXX Ps. 73:12].*
O Thou Who didst form Adam from dust of old, having been smitten on the cheek by a clayey hand, O Merciful One, Thou didst endure crucifixion, mockings and wounds. O the wonder! O of Thy great longsuffering! Glory, O Lord, to Thy life-bearing passions, whereby Thou hast saved us.[^1016]

*Verse: God is wonderful in His Saints, the God of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:36].*
*For the Martyrs:*
With the boast of suffering and the coronal honor have the glorious passion-bearers been clothed by Thee, O Lord; for by the endurance of wounds they overcame the lawless, and by divine power received victory from heaven. By their prayers set us also free from the invisible foe, O Savior, and save us.[^1017]

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee stretched out dead upon the Cross, O Christ, the All-immaculate Mother cried aloud: O my Son, equally unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved, O Compassionate One, the creation of Thine all-pure hands?[^1018]

*After the 3rd Stichologia, Sessional Hymns, Tone 1:*
When the sun beheld Thee, O Word, Sun of glory, Jesus, for the compassion of mercy voluntarily suspended in the flesh upon the tree of the Cross, not enduring the audacity, it was darkened. Therefore enlighten my darkened soul with Thine unapproachable light, and save me, I pray Thee.[^1019]

Thou wast voluntarily nailed upon the Cross, O Compassionate One, and didst deify our corrupt nature, and didst put to death the serpent, murderer of man. But establish the world in Orthodoxy, and cast down the uprisings of heretics by Thy precious Cross.[^1020]

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
O Savior Who by Thy Cross didst conquer enemies, scatter their heresies like dust, and exalt the horn of Thy precious Church; stay, O Lord, the tempest stirred up by them against us, and grant peace unto the multitudes of the Orthodox, through the prayers of her that bare Thee.[^1021]

*Canon to the Honorable and Life-Giving Cross,*
*whose acrostic is: "The precious Passion of the Master do I praise." (A composition of Lord Joseph).*
*Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Unto God Who helped Moses in Egypt, and thereby drowned Pharaoh with all his host, let us sing a song of victory; for He hath been glorified.[^1022]

*Troparion 1:* Having endured the passions for our sake, Thou Who art impassible by nature, and being crucified with two thieves, Thou didst slay the originating author of evil, the serpent, O Word, and hast saved those who worship Thee.[^1023]

*Troparion 2:* Being the Orient of orients, O Jesus, Thou didst come unto our nature cast away toward the west; and the sun, seeing Thee crucified, hid its light.[^1024]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 3:* Nobly exchanging temporal death for life everlasting, O glorious sufferers, ye were accounted worthy of the Heavenly Kingdom; therefore are ye glorified and called blessed.[^1025]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 4:* Having valiantly conformed yourselves to the passions of Christ, ye heal the sufferings of earthly men by mystical operation, and by a word drive away spirits, O holy martyrs.[^1026]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
As an Ewe-lamb, beholding Christ lifted up upon the Cross, and gazing upon Him, the Ewe-lamb cried out, exclaiming: Whither hath Thy beauty set, O longsuffering Son, most unoriginate?[^1027]

*Another Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*
*Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Let us all sing a victorious song unto God Who wrought marvelous wonders with an high arm, and saved Israel; for He hath been glorified.[^1028]

*Troparion 1:* Thou who alone didst bear the timeless God incarnate in time, O all-holy, Most Pure One, heal the lifelong passions of my most wretched soul.[^1029]

*Troparion 2:* The wounds of my soul, and the perplexity of my heart, the darkening of my thoughts, and the defeat of my mind consume, O Most Pure One, as thou art merciful, by thy prayers.[^1030]

*Troparion 3:* O thou who didst bear the Light, my Deliverer, deliver me, O Most Pure One, from darkness and everlasting torments, that, being saved, I may hymn thy tender-heartedness.[^1031]

*Troparion 4:* Drowning in the abyss of evils and in the tumult of passions, I call upon thy calm, O Most Pure One: save me; for thou art the haven of the faithful.[^1032]

*Canon to the Cross, Ode 3. Heirmos:* Let my heart be established in Thy will, O Christ God, Who hast established the second heaven above the waters, and founded the earth upon the waters, O Almighty One.[^1033]

*Troparion 1:* Having spread out Thy palms upon the Cross, Thou didst stain Thy divine fingers with blood, delivering the work of Thy hands, Adam, O Master, from the murderous hand, as Thou alone art good and the Lover of mankind.[^1034]

*Troparion 2:* Thou wast pierced in the side with a spear, O Master, rectifying the slipping of the rib; Thou didst ascend upon the tree, bringing him who received misfortune of old through the fruit of the tree into paradise with the wise thief.[^1035]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 3:* As the pillars of the Church, the foremost bulwarks of piety, the destroyers of enemies, let us in songs bless the Lord's martyrs with pure intent.[^1036]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 4:* Being divine branches of the noetic vine, the martyrs plainly brought forth for us clusters of grapes, pouring forth the wine of endurance, gladdening the hearts of all the faithful.[^1037]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Blessed is the fruit of thy womb, O all-lauded Virgin, Who, for the sake of His Cross, by divine grace hath made partakers of incorruption those corrupted by the fruit of the tree.[^1038]

*Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 3.*
*Troparion 1:* Consume all the barrenness of my unfruitful mind, and show forth my soul fruitful in virtues, O most pure Theotokos, help of the faithful.[^1039]

*Troparion 2:* Deliver me, O All-immaculate One, from every tribulation, from the many snares of the serpent, and from everlasting fire and darkness, O thou who didst give birth to the pre-eternal Light.[^1040]

*Troparion 3:* By the dread judgment-seat, O Pure One, and by that unquenchable fire and the terrible sentence am I wholly condemned; make haste to save me, thy servant, O all-pure Sovereign Lady.[^1041]

*Troparion 4:* That He might deify humanity, God became man from thee, O pure Virgin, beyond speech and comprehension; therefore with one accord do all we the faithful call thee blessed.[^1042]

*Canon to the Cross, Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard Thy report, O Lord, and was afraid; I considered Thy works (the prophet said), and glorified Thy power.[^1043]

*Troparion 1:* Being a righteous Lawgiver, Thou wast reckoned among the lawless; Thou didst ascend upon the tree, O Benefactor and Lord, desiring to justify all.[^1044]

*Troparion 2:* All the angelic powers marvelled, beholding Thee, the Sun, lifted up upon the Cross, and conquering the principalities of darkness.[^1045]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 3:* Having drawn the grace of healings from spiritual gifts, the martyrs wash away from all men soul-corrupting passions by the grace of God.[^1046]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 4:* Having cast away the slumber of negligence, the passion-bearers by the vigilance of divine faith put to sleep the raging assaults of wild beasts, and suffered rejoicing.[^1047]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Woe is me, O Child! How do I behold Thee lifted up upon the tree, and unjustly slain, Who givest life? the Virgin said weeping.[^1048]

*Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4. Heirmos:* Foreseeing in the Spirit the incarnation of the Word, O prophet Habakkuk, thou didst proclaim, crying aloud: When the years draw near, Thou shalt be known; when the time is come, Thou shalt be shown forth. Glory to Thy power, O Lord.[^1049]

*Troparion 1:* O Virgin Theotokos, undefiled tabernacle, cleanse me who am defiled with transgressions, by the purest sprinklings of thy mercies. Give me a hand of help, that I may cry: Glory to thee, O pure Bride of God.[^1050]

*Troparion 2 [to the Theotokos]:* Thou hast been shown forth as a sanctified temple unto God, Who dwelt within thee beyond comprehension. Pray Him to cleanse us from sinful defilements, that we may be known as a temple and a spiritual dwelling-place.[^1051]

*Troparion 3:* Have mercy upon me, O Theotokos, who alone didst give birth to the fountain of mercies, and loosen the grievous sickness and the heartache of my soul; grant me a stream of tears and compunction before the end, and deliverance from evils.[^1052]

*Troparion 4:* O holy Theotokos, sanctify us, who didst give birth in the flesh to the All-Holy One, Who willed to become like unto men; and by thy prayers, O Most Pure One, show all to be partakers of the Heavenly Kingdom.[^1053]

*Canon to the Cross, Ode 5. Heirmos:* Shine forth upon us Thy bright and ever-existing light, who early from the morning seek the judgments of Thy commandments, O Master, Lover of mankind, Christ our God.[^1054]

*Troparion 1:* Being lifted up in the flesh upon the Cross, Thou didst call the nations who knew Thee not unto the knowledge of Thee, O Judge of all, Who alone art merciful, Christ our God.[^1055]

*Troparion 2:* When Thou didst stand before the unjust judgment-seat, O righteous Lord, Adam, who had formerly been condemned, was justified, and cries: Glory to Thy crucifixion, O longsuffering Lord![^1056]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 3:* As a paradise planted by God were ye shown forth, O martyrs, having your precious sufferings as fragrant flowers, whereby every soul of the faithful is filled with fragrance.[^1057]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 4:* As fair-flowering and fruitful trees that have blossomed forth the fruit of faith and immortality and uprooted the roots of malice, let us sing of the Lord's martyrs.[^1058]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The Virgin, the precious rod that budded forth the unfading flower, when she beheld Him lifted up upon the tree, cried aloud: O Master, make me not childless.[^1059]

*Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5. Heirmos:* Grant us Thy peace, O Son of God! for beside Thee we know no other God, we name Thy Name, for Thou art the God of the living and of the dead.[^1060]

*Troparion 1:* The evil tasting in Eden made me dead of old; but O Most Pure One, who didst give birth to Life, make me alive now who died of old by the tree, and grant peace.[^1061]

*Troparion 2:* Save me, O Most Pure One, from grievous troubles, and raise me up from the corruption of passions; and deliver me, thy unprofitable servant, from captivity and the malice of wicked demons.[^1062]

*Troparion 3:* Enlighten the pupils of my soul, O Pure One, that I may ever behold the divine radiance and thy glory, O All-immaculate One, that I may obtain mercy and everlasting glory.[^1063]

*Troparion 4:* We know thee as a cloud, and paradise, and the gate of light, the table and the fleece, the jar bearing the manna within, the sweetness of the world, O pure Virgin Mother.[^1064]

*Canon to the Cross, Ode 6. Heirmos:* Thou didst save the prophet from the whale, O Lover of mankind! And raise me up from the depth of transgressions, I pray Thee.[^1065]

*Troparion 1:* Thou Who art higher than all honor didst endure dishonor, O Christ, lifted up upon the Cross, desiring to honor men.[^1066]

*Troparion 2:* Thou dost sign a pardon for me who was enslaved by the deceiver, being struck with a reed, O Christ our all-good God.[^1067]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 3:* Through the pains of sufferings, O saints, ye passed over unto a painless end, being accounted worthy of ineffable joy.[^1068]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 4:* Ye were kindled with the coals of Christ's love, O most wise ones! Therefore, cast into the fire, ye remained unconsumed.[^1069]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
After giving birth, O All-immaculate One, thou didst remain even as thou wast before birth; for thou didst bear God, Who by the tree hath saved mankind.[^1070]

*Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 6. Heirmos:* Imitating the prophet Jonah, I cry aloud: Deliver my life from corruption, O Good One, and save me, O Savior of the world, who cry: Glory to Thee.[^1071]

*Troparion 1:* Defiled as I am by many sins, I pray thee, who alone art the good and undefiled tabernacle: wash me clean from every defilement through thine intercession.[^1072]

*Troparion 2:* Be thou a pilot unto me, O Pure One, tossed about by worldly necessities in the deep of evils; and guide me unto a calm harbor, and save me.[^1073]

*Troparion 3:* The surging tempest of thoughts, the assaults of passions, and the depth of sin toss like waves my wretched soul; help me, O holy Sovereign Lady.[^1074]

*Troparion 4:* O Mary, shown forth as the sacred tabernacle, sanctify my wretched soul which is defiled by pleasures.[^1075]

*Canon to the Cross, Ode 7. Heirmos:* The furnace was bedewed, O Savior, and the children, dancing, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^1076]

*Troparion 1:* Being crucified, Thou didst shake creation; being put to death, Thou didst slay the serpent. Blessed art Thou, O Christ, the God of our fathers.[^1077]

*Troparion 2:* The Long-suffering One is given gall to drink, welling forth saving sweetness unto me, who through sweet food was deprived of the sustenance of Paradise.[^1078]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 3:* Scraped with claws, the martyrs, having laid aside the coarseness of mortality, received divine beauty from God.[^1079]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 4:* Likening yourselves by your sufferings unto the most pure Passion of Christ, O valiant martyrs, ye readily endured the wounds inflicted by the enemy.[^1080]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lord crucified, the spotless Theotokos said: Woe is me, O my Son! How art Thou dying, O Life and Hope of the faithful?[^1081]

*Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7. Other Heirmos:* The fire touched not nor troubled Thy children in the furnace, O Savior. Then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: Blessed is the God of our fathers.[^1082]

*Troparion 1:* Sanctify my soul defiled by passions, O most pure Bride of God; and quickly destroy the grievous captivity of my mind, the perplexity of my heart, and the assaults of demons, O Pure One.[^1083]

*Troparion 2:* Slain by carnal passions, O All-immaculate One, quicken my mind and strengthen me to perform things well-pleasing unto God, that I may magnify thee and ever glorify thy tender compassion.[^1084]

*Troparion 3:* O Virgin Mother, who alone didst bear God, put to death my carnal pleasures, and quickly wash away the defilement of my soul; and deliver me from the torment of wicked demons, and save me.[^1085]

*Troparion 4:* Adorned with divine virtues, O pure Virgin, thou didst bear the Word co-unoriginate with the Father, Who truly covered the heavens with virtues; Whom do thou ever implore to have compassion on us.[^1086]

*Canon to the Cross, Ode 8. Heirmos:* Hymn Christ God, O singers, Who saved the children in the furnace, and changed the thunderous flame into dew; and highly exalt Him unto all ages.[^1087]

*Troparion 1:* When Thou wast nailed to the Cross, O Savior, creation was shaken...[^1088]

...the sun withheld its rays, the rocks were split, and Hades was swiftly laid bare, not enduring Thy dominion.[^1089]

*Troparion 2:* For the condemnation of the outcast, Thou didst suffer condemnation; for him that received nakedness, Thou didst hang naked upon the tree, O Compassionate One! Great is Thy dominion and longsuffering.[^1090]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 3:* Companions of the bodiless hosts, soldiers of Christ, armed with the Cross as with breastplates, deployed against the adversary, trampling him underfoot with beautiful feet.[^1091]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 4:* In the midst of the tribunal the valiant ones stood with bonds, being torn limb from limb, tearing down the edifice of delusion and smashing the shrines of demons.[^1092]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
She that is higher than the heavens, beholding the only Exalted One lifted up upon the tree, and casting down the arrogance of the enemy, sang aloud unto Christ.[^1093]

*Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 8. Other Heirmos:* Him of Whom angels and all hosts are in dread as Creator and Lord, sing, ye priests; glorify, ye children; bless, ye peoples; and highly exalt Him unto all ages.[^1094]

*Troparion 1:* The Bodiless One is divinely incarnate from thee; pray Him, O Most Pure One, to put to death my carnal passions, and to quicken my soul which hath been slain by sins.[^1095]

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst bear the Savior and God, O Most Pure One, Who heals the brokenness of earthly Adam; beseech Him to heal the wounds of my soul, which suffer incurably.[^1096]

*Troparion 3:* Raise me up who lie in the depth of evils, and overcome him who now fights against me, O Pure One; and despise not my soul wounded by improper pleasures, O Most Pure One, but have compassion and save me.[^1097]

*Troparion 4:* We are delivered from all manner of temptations, O Most Pure One, through thy vigilant prayers unto God, knowing thee to be the blessed and full of grace Theotokos.[^1098]

*Then: My soul doth magnify the Lord: and prostrations.*[^1099]

*Canon to the Cross, Ode 9. Heirmos:* Thee, whom Moses saw as the unconsumed bush, and the animate ladder which Jacob saw, and the heavenly gate through which Christ our God passed, with hymns, O pure Mother, do we magnify.[^1100]

*Troparion 1:* O how did the disobedient people deliver Thee to the Cross, the only Long-...[^1101]

...suffering One! Who willingly becamest poor, and didst accept the Passion, and becamest the Mediator of impassibility unto all who had slipped from Adam.[^1102]

*Troparion 2:* An ignominious crucifixion didst Thou accept in the flesh, O Christ, desiring to honor man dishonored by irrational passions, who had lost his ancient beauty. Glory beyond mind to Thy tender mercy![^1103]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 3:* O Christ, unsetting Sun, those that rose early toward Thee and traversed the darkness of sufferings by Thy beckoning, hast Thou guided to the light of Thine ineffable glory and brightness; therefore enlighten us by their prayers.[^1104]

*For the Martyrs:*
*Troparion 4:* The regiment of holy martyrs overcame the hosts of noetic enemies, and was joined to the ten thousands of holy powers; and by an all-efficacious sign it ever heals the darkness of passions of our souls.[^1105]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
With the light of the Light that shone forth from thee in the flesh, O Virgin, illumine my mind and enlighten my heart, driving away the darkness of sin, and dispersing all the mist of my despondency.[^1106]

*Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 9. Other Heirmos:* The light-bearing cloud, into which the Master of all, as rain from heaven upon a fleece, came down, and was incarnate for our sake, becoming man, the Unoriginate One, all we magnify as the pure Mother of our God.[^1107]

*Troparion 1:* Loving sin, I abide in laziness, and I tremble before the inexorable tribunal, O Pure One; whereat do thou preserve me uncondemned through thy holy prayers, O Bride of God, that as my protectress I may call thee blessed, O Most Pure One.[^1108]

*Troparion 2:* I am in dread of the tribunal, O Virgin, and of the unforgetting eye of thy Son, having committed many sins upon the earth; and therefore I cry unto thee: O all-compassionate Sovereign Lady, help me, and deliver me from the trouble of that time, and save me, O Pure One.[^1109]

*Troparion 3:* How dread is the day of examination, O Maiden! How terrible the answer! How bitter the calamity! Who will stand henceforth? O most pure Sovereign Lady, have mercy on my passionate soul, and grant me remission before the end, O Most Pure One.[^1110]

*Troparion 4:* O thou who didst bear the divine Light that shone forth from the Father, my soul darkened by the deceits...[^1111]

...of this life, and which hath become a mockery unto enemies, do thou pity, O All-immaculate One, and account worthy of the light of saving repentance, O Pure One.[^1112]

*Exaposteilarion.*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "The sky with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
O Cross of my Christ, scepter and victory of rulers, boast of the Orthodox, preserve those who bow down before thee, that the multitudes of blasphemous men may be vanquished.[^1113]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Cross, Tone 1:*
Thee Who wast nailed upon the tree in the flesh, and Who hast granted us life, as Savior and Master do we sing unceasingly.[^1114]

*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced and were glad: - - - And look upon Thy servants, and upon the works of Thy hands, and guide their sons.*[^1115]
By Thy Cross, O Christ, there hath become one flock of angels and of men, and in one assembly heaven and earth rejoice. O Lord, glory to Thee.

*Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us: - - And direct Thou the works of our hands upon us; yea, the work of our hands do Thou direct.*[^1116]
*For the Martyrs:*
Come, all ye peoples, let us honor with chants and spiritual songs the passion-bearers of Christ: the luminaries of the world and preachers of the faith, the ever-flowing fountains from which healings pour forth unto the faithful. By their prayers, O Christ our God, grant peace to Thy world, and to our souls great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченици):*
When the Ewe-lamb, the Ewe-lamb and all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, beheld upon the Cross Him Who had neither form nor beauty, woe is me! weeping she said: Whither hath Thy beauty set, O sweetest One? Whither Thy majesty? Whither the radiant splendor of Thine image, O my most beloved Son?[^1117]

*On Friday, The Beatitudes, Tone 1:*
By food the enemy led Adam out of Paradise, but by the Cross Christ brought the thief into it, who cried: Remember me when Thou comest into Thy kingdom.[^1118]

Being crucified, O Sinless One, Thou didst take upon Thyself the sins of all, O Christ, and being pierced in the side, Thou didst pour forth the streams of salvation: water...[^1119]

...and blood, building up those crushed by corruption.[^1120]

Being nailed to the tree of Thine own will, O Jesus God, Thou didst take away, O Compassionate One, all the passionate understanding of Adam; and by Thy precious wounds Thou didst wound the demonic host.[^1121]

*For the Martyrs:*
Likening yourselves unto the sufferings of Him Who willingly suffered in the flesh, O glorious martyrs, ye ever heal incurable passions, and drive away diseases from men by spiritual power.[^1122]

*Glory...*
*Triadikon:* As equal in power and equally honored Trinity do we glorify Thee: the unoriginate God the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, one tri-hypostatic Godhead, magnifying in faith.[^1123]

*Both now...*
*Stavrotheotokion:* When she that bare Thee in the flesh beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Christ God, weeping she said: What hath the ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, O my Son?[^1124]

## Saturday: All Saints and the Departed[^1125]

*On Friday Evening.*
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerables:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченици):*

*Sticheron 1:* The martyrs in unified festivity, by the streams of their blood drowned the multitude of demons, and brought to an end all shameful sacrifices; they destroyed idolatrous delusion, having patiently suffered, and now they pray unto Christ to grant our souls peace and great mercy.[^1126]

*Sticheron 2:* The all-sacred shepherds, with wise words and teachings, taught all to glorify the Trinity in the unity of the Godhead, divinely avoiding the confusion and division of the Persons; and now they pray that there may be granted to our souls peace and great mercy.[^1127]

*Sticheron 3:* The company of the venerable put to sleep carnal desires, and bridled their impulses, showing forth an angelic life. Therefore in the heavenly mansions do they now make merry, praying...[^1128]

...unto Christ to grant our souls peace and great mercy.[^1129]

*Other Stichera, to the Martyrs, Same Tone:*
*Sticheron 1:* By the prayers of all the saints and of the Theotokos, grant us Thy peace, O Lord, and have mercy on us, as Thou alone art compassionate.[^1130]

*Sticheron 2:* Your confession before the tribunal, O saints, spat upon the power of demons and freed men from delusion. Therefore also, as your heads were being cut off, ye cried: Let the sacrifice of our souls be acceptable before Thee, O Lord, for, having loved Thee, we took no thought for temporal life, O Lover of mankind.[^1131]

*Sticheron 3:* O goodly traffic of yours, O saints! For ye gave blood and inherited the heavens, and being tried for a time, ye rejoice eternally. Truly goodly is your traffic; for leaving corruptible things, ye received incorruptible, and keeping festival with the angels, ye sing unceasingly unto the consubstantial Trinity.[^1132]

*Glory... Both now... Dogmatikon Theotokion:*
Let us sing of Mary the Virgin, the boast of the whole world, who blossomed forth from mankind and bare the Master, the heavenly gate, the song of the bodiless hosts, and the adornment of the faithful. For she appeared as heaven and temple of the Godhead; she, having broken down the barrier of enmity, brought in peace and opened the Kingdom. Having her, therefore, as the confirmation of our faith, we have as champion the Lord Who was born of her. Be of good courage therefore, be of good courage, O people of God, for He will conquer the enemies, for He is all-powerful.[^1133]

*At the Aposticha, Martyrikon, Tone 1:*
O all-lauded martyrs, the earth did not hide you, but heaven received you, and the gates of Paradise were opened unto you; and being within, ye enjoy the Tree of Life. Pray unto Christ to grant our souls peace and great mercy.[^1134]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.*[^1135]
*For the Departed:*
What earthly sweetness remains unpartaker of sorrow? What glory stands immutable upon earth? All things are more feeble than shadows, all things more deceitful than dreams; in a single hour death takes all these things away. But in the light of Thy countenance, O Christ, and in the enjoyment of Thy beauty, give rest unto them whom Thou hast chosen, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*[^1136]
*For the Departed:*
No one is without sin, none among men hath been such, save Thou alone, O Immortal One. Therefore, as compassionate God, establish Thy servants in light with Thy choirs of angels, in Thy tender...[^1137]

...compassion, overlooking transgressions, and granting them forgiveness.[^1138]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Truly beyond mind are the marvelous majesties of thy childbearing, O Bride of God, which all the prophets proclaimed, all most glorious: the conception and the birth, O All-hymned One, incomprehensible and ineffable, whereby He hath saved the world, as He is merciful.[^1139]

*On Friday at Compline.*
*Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*
*Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Let us all sing a victorious song unto God Who wrought marvelous wonders with an high arm, and saved Israel; for He hath been glorified.[^1140]

*Refrain: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*
*Troparion 1:* A saving harbor and protection art thou unto those who call upon thee, O most pure Theotokos! Therefore I also from my soul call upon thee fervently: O Sovereign Lady, save me.[^1141]

*Troparion 2:* O pure Mary, Mother of the Creator, heal my humble soul decayed by passions and transgressions, as thou art good and propitious.[^1142]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* O unwedded Sovereign Lady, cease not to pray ever unto my Creator and God, on behalf of him that ever flees unto thy protection, that I may obtain mercy.[^1143]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Animate palace of the King, and fiery throne, with the holy martyrs and the Apostles ever pray Christ to deliver us from tribulations.[^1144]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* The stone which the builders rejected, the same is become the head of the corner. This is the stone upon which Christ hath established the Church, which He redeemed from the nations.[^1145]

*Troparion 1:* The darkness of transgressions holds fast my heart, O Sovereign Lady, and I dare not look up unto heaven with my eyes. Therefore I cry: Enlighten my mind and my heart with the commandments of Christ.[^1146]

*Troparion 2:* I gaze upon thy divine icon, and I honor the Most Pure Mother as the prototype; and this I kiss, venerate, and praise, knowing the honor of thee and of Him to be one.[^1147]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Restrain the fierce bodily impulses, and quench the flame of passions, O Virgin, wherewith the manifold woven chain of sins...[^1148]

...the fierce serpent weavs for me, desiring to snatch me away unto destruction.[^1149]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Wholly clothed in Adam from thee, O Theotokos, the Word came forth, taking on the whole body. Pray Him, therefore, to deliver us from passions, and from various temptations, and from everlasting fire.[^1150]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Foreseeing in spirit the incarnation of the Word, O prophet Habakkuk, thou didst proclaim, crying: When the years draw near, Thou shalt be known; when the time is come, Thou shalt be shown forth. Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^1151]

*Troparion 1:* Having given birth to the Creator and Maker of all, thou hast ineffable power and invincible might, O Virgin, and thou savest those who come unto thee in faith; therefore I cry unto thee: O Sovereign Lady of the world, help me.[^1152]

*Troparion 2:* Cast down the warfare of invisible, noetic enemies, O Sovereign Lady, who ever assault my wretched heart, granting divine tranquility and peace, that I may joyfully hymn thee.[^1153]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Hope of the hopeless, helper of the poor, consolation of those who mourn, cleansing of those who sin, guide of the wandering, physician of the sick, and restoration of the falling.[^1154]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* O pure and blessed Virgin Mary, who alone didst change the curse of all! With the Apostles, martyrs, and prophets, pray Him Who came forth from thy womb, to save the souls of those who hymn thee.[^1155]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Grant us Thy peace, O Son of God; for we know no other God save Thee, we name Thy Name, for Thou art the God of the living and of the dead.[^1156]

*Troparion 1:* Save me, O Sovereign Lady, from the depth of temptations, tribulations, sorrows, and bodily passions, and preserve my soul in divine tranquility.[^1157]

*Troparion 2:* Thou art my fervent protection and firm defense in all my life, O All-immaculate One! Therefore I pray thee: Extend unto me thy rich tender compassion after death also.[^1158]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* O thou who didst give birth to the Creator and Redeemer of all: Deliver me from bodily passions which burn my heart and draw me unto unfitting deeds.[^1159]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Having appeared as the place of noetic sanctification, sanctify me wholly...[^1160]

...O Sovereign Lady, and with the holy and wise Apostles pray, O Virgin, that I may be saved.[^1161]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Imitating the prophet Jonah, I cry aloud: Deliver my life from corruption, O Good One, and save me, O Savior of the world, who cry: Glory to Thee![^1162]

*Troparion 1:* Thou dost preserve me alive, delivering me from temptations; do thou also translate me when departing from the earth unto everlasting life, O Virgin Mother, by thy tender compassion.[^1163]

*Troparion 2:* Thou art an unbreakable wall, thou art a firm protection, O good Theotokos, thou art a strong protectress unto thy servant; and therefore do I ever call upon thee.[^1164]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having acquired powerful intercession with God, O good Theotokos, despise me not who fervently flee unto thy protection, and cry aloud: Have mercy on me, O Mother of the God of all.[^1165]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* Cast me not away, O Christ; abhor me not, O Savior; the Ewe-lamb that bare Thee in the flesh prays Thee, with Thine Apostles, prophets, and passion-bearers.[^1166]

*Then: Lord, have mercy, thrice. Glory... Both now...*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*
A cloud of the co-eternal divine Light, O Virgin, and an ark, and a lampstand, and an urn, and an unhewn mountain, did the prophets foretell thee to be, O most pure Maiden! For from thee seedlessly in the latter times came forth Christ our God, even as the Father was well pleased.[^1167]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The fire touched not nor troubled Thy children in the furnace, O Savior. Then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: Blessed is the God of our fathers.[^1168]

*Troparion 1:* When I am in despair, O Pure One, through transgressions and carnal pleasures, and am driven into the depth of perdition, come quickly and deliver me, O most pure Sovereign Lady, by thy tender compassion.[^1169]

*Troparion 2:* A mighty refuge, and protection, and deliverance unto all the faithful art thou, O Theotokos; be thou a guide unto me toward the Creator, granting me deliverance from transgressions by thy tender-heartedness.[^1170]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Thou didst destroy the curse, O Pure One, who didst bear Christ our Joy; do thou also loose by thy power the curse that hath come upon me from sin, O All-immaculate One, and grant me joy.[^1171]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* We thy servants pray thee, O Most Holy Pure One, ever by day and by night, praying with contrite mind: Grant deliverance from our transgressions through thy prayers.[^1172]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Him of Whom angels and all hosts are in dread as Creator and Lord, sing, ye priests; glorify, ye children; bless, ye peoples; and highly exalt Him unto all ages.[^1173]

*Troparion 1:* Arise, anticipate, war against the enemies who ever trouble and afflict us, manifesting thy power, O holy Virgin, helper of the world.[^1174]

*Troparion 2:* Thou hast saved the world from death and corruption by thy divine childbearing, O good Virgin; and now by thy prayers snatch us away from passions and save us who praise thee.[^1175]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Great and ineffable is thy might, O Virgin, manifold and invincible are thy divine mercies and tender compassion. Therefore save us who truly call upon thee.[^1176]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* As an animate vine, O Virgin, thou didst put forth for us the ripe Cluster that welled forth the wine of forgiveness, and dried up the drunkenness of sin.[^1177]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* The light-bearing cloud, into which the Master of all, as rain from heaven upon a fleece, came down, and was incarnate for our sake, becoming man, the Unoriginate One, all we magnify as the pure Mother of our God.[^1178]

*Troparion 1:* Buffeted by carnal pleasures in the sea of life, I fall down before thee and cry aloud: Have mercy on me who flee unto thee, O Sovereign Lady, and stretch forth to me a hand of salvation, delivering me from the deep of perdition.[^1179]

*Troparion 2:* Unto thee do I confess my sins, O Virgin! Unto thy face do I present my shame, and I cry aloud from my soul: Have mercy and show compassion, O Most Pure One, for after God I have placed my firm hope in thee.[^1180]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Thee who seedlessly didst bear the Creator and Master, I call upon from my soul, and I pray diligently: Save me from the corruption of many transgressions, and deliver me from unquenchable fire, O most holy, good Theotokos.[^1181]

*Both now...*
*Theotokion:* O impassable gate, portal of Paradise, way of those who are saved, path of salvation, with the martyrs and...[^1182]

...the Prophets, with the Righteous and the Venerable, and with the divine Apostles, pray, O Pure One, that our souls may be saved.[^1183]

*Then: It is truly meet: Trisagion and the rest.*[^1184]

*On Saturday at Matins.*
*After the 1st Stichologia, Sessional Hymns, to the Martyrs, Tone 1:*
With the boast of suffering and eternal honor, O glorious passion-bearers, having clothed yourselves with Thee, O Lord, by the endurance of wounds they conquered the lawless, and received victory from heaven by divine power. By their prayers deliver us also from the invisible enemy, O Savior, and save us.[^1185]

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^1186]
As good soldiers of one mind in faith, ye feared not the threats of the torturers, O holy ones; ye came fervently unto Christ, having taken up the precious Cross, and having finished your course, ye received victory from heaven. Glory to Him Who hath strengthened you, glory to Him Who hath crowned you, glory to Him Who through you works healings for all.[^1187]

*Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Спа́се):*
*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*[^1188]
In a place of light, in the choir of the righteous, give rest, O Savior, unto those who have passed over unto Thee; for they have placed their hope in Thee, O Lover of mankind. Accept our supplication for the fathers and children whose memory we celebrate, and justify them, as Thou art abundant in mercy.[^1189]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
When Gabriel said unto thee: Rejoice! O Virgin, with that voice the Master of all was incarnate in thee, the holy Ark, as the righteous David said; thou didst appear more spacious than the heavens, having borne Thy Creator. Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee, glory to Him Who came forth from thee, glory to Him Who hath set us free through thy childbearing.[^1190]

*After the 2nd Stichologia, Sessional Hymns, Tone 1:*
By the wounds of the saints who suffered for Thy sake, be Thou entreated, O Lord, and heal all our sicknesses and passions, O Lover of mankind, we pray Thee.[^1191]

*Verse: Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath magnified all His desires in them.*[^1192]

Let us all beseech the martyrs of Christ, and let us come unto them in love with faith; for they intercede for our salvation, and they all pour forth the grace of healings; they drive away the regiments of demons, as guardians of the faith.[^1193]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.*[^1194]
Having abolished the dominion of death, O Christ, Thou didst well forth incorruption unto those on earth; and they that believe in Thee die not, but live ever in Thee. Give rest therefore, O Lord, unto the souls of Thy servants, and appoint them with Thy saints, by the prayers of the Theotokos, granting them Thy mercy.[^1195]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O only firm and fervent protectress of the human race, O Virgin Theotokos, cease not to pray, with the prophets, and martyrs, with the hierarchs, and fasters, and venerable ones, unto God the Word, Whom thou didst bear beyond nature, to save all creation.[^1196]

*Canon to the Holy Martyrs, and Hierarchs, the Venerable, and the Departed, whose acrostic is: Unto those who have loved God, I sing a divine song. (A work of Joseph).*[^1197]
*Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Guiding Israel with a pillar of fire and cloud, as God He parted the sea, and covered Pharaoh's chariots with the deep. Let us sing a song of victory, for He alone hath been glorified.[^1198]

*Refrain: Holy Martyrs, pray to God for us.*[^1199]
*Troparion 1:* Protected by the shield of piety, the divine sufferers rushed unto the contest; and they destroyed all the power of the enemy, singing a song of victory unto Christ Who strengthened them.[^1200]

*Refrain: Holy ones of Christ, pray to God for us.*[^1201]
*Troparion 2:* Ye shepherded the flock of God upon mystical pasture, O divine pastors, driving away the wolves with the staff of sacred words; and rejoicing, ye took up your abode in the heavenly fold, where the Great Shepherd is.[^1202]

*Refrain: All ye Saints, pray to God for us.*[^1203]
*Troparion 3:* Having put to death the passions of the flesh by fasting and sufferings, by the divine nod ye live after death, O Venerable ones; ever pray Christ, Who for our sake became dead, to have compassion upon us.[^1204]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^1205]
*Troparion 4:* Having become dead, Thou didst grant unto the dead Thy divine life and immortal existence; those who have passed over in faith from this cor-...[^1206]

...ruptible life, make partakers of Thy kingdom, as Thou art full of tender compassion, and alone abundant in mercy.[^1207]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The martyrs wounded by the love of Christ, and the women crowned by God, and all the venerable ones honor thee, the all-immaculate and fair among women, faithfully rejoicing, as the all-honorable Queen.[^1208]

*Second Canon for the Departed.*
*We sing it in order, where there is no Menaion, or at the office for the departed, over the kolyvo.*[^1209]
*(Whose acrostic is: Unto those asleep in the faith, I bring the first song).*[^1210]
*Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Thy victorious right hand hath been glorified in power in a manner befitting God; for it, O Immortal One, as all-powerful, shattered the adversaries, fashioning a new path through the deep for the Israelites.[^1211]

*Refrain: Marvelous is God in His saints.*[^1212]
*Troparion 1:* The gates of death and its bars hast Thou shattered by Thy death, O Immortal One! Open, O Master, the gates of immortality beyond mind unto the departed, by the prayers of Thy passion-bearers.[^1213]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of the departed.*[^1214]
*Troparion 2:* That we might be accounted worthy of divine life, Thou didst descend unto death, and having overturned its treasuries, Thou didst draw us forth from thence; and now, O Giver of life, give rest unto the departed.[^1215]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having assumed my corruptible and mortal nature, Thou hast clothed it in incorruption, and hast lifted it up unto unending and blessed life; where, as Thou art compassionate, give rest unto them whom Thou hast received.[^1216]

*Theotokion:*
Let us the faithful sing of her who bare the divine Word from God; for that all-pure one became the way of life unto the dead, whom as the Receiver of God and Theotokos we glorify.[^1217]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Let my heart be established in Thy will, O Christ God, Who didst establish the second heaven above the waters, and didst found the earth upon the waters, O Almighty One.[^1218]

*Refrain: Holy Martyrs, pray to God for us.*[^1219]
*Troparion 1:* Having endured many torments, ye were accounted worthy of many good things, O sacred company of martyrs. Therefore by your prayers cleanse the immeasurable multitude of my evils.[^1220]

*Refrain: Holy ones of Christ, pray to God for us.*[^1221]
*Troparion 2:* Clothed with righteous grace, and having acquired the beautiful joy of the choir of the venerable, the sacred ones were likened unto the immaterial ministers.[^1222]

*Refrain: All ye Saints, pray to God for us.*[^1223]

*Troparion 3:* O prophets of Christ, divine martyrs, assembly of holy women who suffered valiantly and were glorified in fasting: by their prayers grant Thy mercies unto all, O Savior.[^1224]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^1225]
*Troparion 4:* O Christ, Who of Thine own will didst fashion man from the earth with Thine own hand, account all Thy servants who have passed over from us in faith worthy of heavenly good things.[^1226]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God who knewest not wedlock, bring the supplications of all unto God our Creator, Who was born of thy womb; that we may find complete deliverance from cruel evils.[^1227]

*Other Heirmos:* O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and didst mercifully take form therein, gird me with power from on high, that I may cry aloud unto Thee: Holy is the animate temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind![^1228]

*Troparion 1:* As Thou alone art good, as Thou alone art abundant in mercy, give rest in heavenly mansions unto those who have piously departed unto Thee, where is ineffable joy and delight, and where the choir of martyrs rejoices.[^1229]

*Troparion 2:* Thou alone didst appear on earth sinless, O my Savior, Who takest away the sins of the world, as Thou art merciful; give rest unto the souls of those who have departed from this world in faith, in Thy holy courts, in the sweetness of Paradise, O Lover of mankind.[^1230]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having cast down the dominion of death, Thou didst well forth unending life unto all the faithful, O Master; appoint therein therefore those who have piously departed, overlooking their immeasurable transgressions and forgiving their sins, O Lover of mankind.[^1231]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Without seed didst thou conceive, O Pure One, the co-eternal Word, Who came unto us in the flesh, and broke down the might of death, and by His tender compassion grants resurrection and eternal life unto the dead.[^1232]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard Thy report, O Lord, and was afraid; I understood Thy works (the Prophet said) and I glorified Thy power.[^1233]

*Troparion 1:* Having endured all manner of wounds with valiant courage, O martyrs, ye were brought unto Christ like blameless sheep, unto the Life that was slain for all.[^1234]

*Troparion 2:* Having become divine mouths, instructed by the Word of God, the hierarchs delivered souls from the mouth of the deceiver, whom we honor with pious mind.[^1235]

*Troparion 3:* Having become strong through the Divine Spirit, O divine fathers, ye strongly conquered the wicked spirits by grace, O Venerable ones.[^1236]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 4:* Having willingly been put to death upon the tree, O our Life, account those who have passed over in faith worthy of eternal life, O Lover of mankind.[^1237]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The company of women denied not the Lord, suffering valiantly, and the holy ones were not conquered by bodily pleasures, having thee as their helper, O Most Pure One.[^1238]

*Other Heirmos:* Looking with foresighted eyes upon thee, the mountain overshadowed by the grace of God, Habakkuk proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel should come forth from thee, unto our salvation and renewal.[^1239]

*Troparion 1:* Having slain Hades by Thine invincible power, Thou wast reckoned among the dead, Thou Who alone art free, O Christ! Free the souls of the pious from condemnation therein, by the prayers of the holy martyrs.[^1240]

*Troparion 2:* Being Master of all, Thou didst give a ransom and price commensurate for those of Adam, even Thine own slaying; therefore we pray Thy compassions: give rest unto the departed, granting remission of sins.[^1241]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Thou didst accept burial in the tomb, O Savior, and as God Thou didst resurrect the dead who were condemned to dwell in graves; now also account the departed worthy of everlasting life, as Thou alone art the Lover of mankind.[^1242]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The human race hath been saved through thy childbearing; for thou didst bear unto us hypostatic Life, the destruction of death, and the ascent that effects life, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, Mother of God.[^1243]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Shine forth upon us Thy bright, eternal light, who rise early unto the judgments of Thy commandments, O Master, Lover of mankind, Christ our God.[^1244]

*Troparion 1:* Armed with piety, O martyrs, ye appeared unwounded by the arrows of the combatants; and having become victorious by grace, ye received crowns.[^1245]

*Troparion 2:* The Lord anointed with divine oil His priests who shepherded the multitudes of the faithful in holiness, and guided them unto the heavenly fold.[^1246]

*Troparion 3:* O most sacred hieromartyrs, and all ye venerable ones, who kept the statutes of the Spirit and became partakers of the kingdom, ye are divinely glorified.[^1247]

*Glory... For the Departed:*
*Troparion 4:* Those whom Thou hast now translated by Thy command, O Master and Lord, account worthy of Thy kingdom with the saints, overlooking all their former transgressions.[^1248]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The women who by fasting and suffering found heavenly glory, O Most Pure One, having thee as their protectress, destroyed the power of the serpent.[^1249]

*Other Heirmos:* O Thou Who didst enlighten by the radiance of Thy coming, O Christ, and didst illumine the ends of the world by Thy Cross, enlighten the hearts with the light of the knowledge of God of those who sing to Thee in Orthodox fashion.[^1250]

*Troparion 1:* Healing us from the deadly venom, Thou didst accept death, and didst blunt the sting of death; do Thou Thyself give rest unto those whom Thou hast received, O Giver of life, by the prayers of the martyrs.[^1251]

*Troparion 2:* Having freed mankind from slaying and corruption, appoint the souls of those who have departed in faith in the courts of Thy saints, whence all sorrow hath fled away and joy hath taken up its abode.[^1252]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* As Thou didst open Paradise unto him who was hanged with Thee, now accept the souls, O Master, of those who have passed over unto Thee in faith, granting them to take up their abode in the church of the firstborn.[^1253]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Delivering those who hold fast their hope in thee, guide them calmly unto the haven of the divine will, by thy maternal boldness toward thy Son, O blessed All-immaculate One.[^1254]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Imitating the prophet Jonah, I cry aloud: Deliver my life from corruption, O Good One, and save me, O Savior of the world, who cry: Glory to Thee![^1255]

*Troparion 1:* Having been put to death by the infliction of many wounds, ye inherited true life, together praying that we all may be saved, O holy martyrs.[^1256]

*Troparion 2:* Ye were known as radiant morning-stars upon the earth, illumining the faithful with the light of piety, O most...[^1257]

...glorious warriors of Christ and all-wise hierarchs.[^1258]

*Troparion 3:* Ye appeared as strangers upon earth, but citizens of heaven, O God-bearing fasters, having put to death the carnal mind through abstinence and humility.[^1259]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 4:* Make those of the faithful whom Thou hast translated partakers of Thine unwaning light and true sweetness, O Lover of mankind, and number them with the assemblies of Thy saints.[^1260]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having suffered valiantly and fasted fervently, the women were brought after thee, O Mother of God, as unto the Queen who stood at the right hand of Christ according to the psalm.[^1261]

*Other Heirmos:* The final abyss hath surrounded us, and there is none to deliver; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter. Save Thy people, O our God; for Thou art the strength of the ailing, and their restoration.[^1262]

*Troparion 1:* As Thou art compassionate, grant forgiveness of transgressions unto the departed, bestowing eternal enjoyment, where the brightness of Thy countenance shines and illumines Thy passion-bearers.[^1263]

*Troparion 2:* O Christ, Who didst redeem the world with the blood that flowed from Thy side: by Thy precious Passion deliver those who have fallen asleep in faith; for Thou didst give Thyself as a ransom for all.[^1264]

*Troparion 3:* O Thou Who in the beginning didst fashion me with Thy most pure hands, and didst bestow a spirit, and when I had fallen evilly to the earth didst renew me more beautifully: do Thou Thyself now give rest unto the souls of the departed.[^1265]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 4:* Show those who have fallen asleep in faith toward Thee to be dwellers in Thy light-bearing bridal chamber, O Lord, overlooking their transgressions, as Thou art good, merciful, and abundant in mercy.[^1266]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
We sing of thee, O blessed Pure One, for whose sake the unwaning Sun of righteousness shone forth upon us who were in darkness and the shadow of death; for thou wast the mediatress of our salvation.[^1267]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The fire touched not nor troubled Thy children in the furnace, O Savior. Then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: Blessed is the God of our fathers.[^1268]

*Troparion 1:* Tested like gold in the fire by all manner of torments, O martyrs...[^1269]

...brighter than any gold, ye appeared in divine love, and were preserved in heavenly treasuries.[^1270]

*Troparion 2:* Having become priests, as divine ministers, all ye holy hierarchs offered unbloody sacrifices unto God; and having shepherded the people, ye took up your abode where the Great Shepherd is.[^1271]

*Troparion 3:* Not submitting to the passions of the flesh, but clothed with dispassion as with a mystical robe, O Venerable ones, ye appeared as companions of the angels; by their prayers, O Christ, deliver us from temptations.[^1272]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 4:* Where the food is mystical, where the light of Thy countenance shines, O Christ, settle those who have passed over from us in faith, O Benefactor, that they may piously praise Thy goodness.[^1273]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having thee, O all-holy Most Pure One, as their beauty, the holy women joyfully dance with the angels, and glorify God the Word Who was born of thee in the flesh, because of His great compassion.[^1274]

*Other Heirmos:* Thee, the spiritual furnace, O Theotokos, do we the faithful contemplate; for as the Exalted One saved the three children, He renewed the world, being wholly in thy womb, the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.[^1275]

*Troparion 1:* Grant, O Christ, that those who have departed unto Thee from the worldly storm may be illumined with the radiances of Thy most pure glory, and account them worthy to cry aloud unto Thee with the martyrs: Blessed art Thou, O praised God of our fathers![^1276]

*Troparion 2:* A new Adam in truth didst Thou become, O Creator of Adam! For Thou alone didst destroy the curse of Adam. Therefore we pray Thee: give rest unto the departed in the sweetness of Paradise, O Christ, as Thou alone art merciful.[^1277]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* O Thou Who alone knowest our natural weakness, as Thou art good and merciful, appoint all whom Thou hast translated where the unsetting light of Thy countenance visits them, O Christ, the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.[^1278]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The shadows of the Law and the ancient riddles passed away through thy childbearing, O Mother of God; for thou didst shine forth upon us the light of divine grace, whereby we have been delivered from ancient debts, O Pure One, singing unto the supremely glorified God.[^1279]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Him of Whom angels and all hosts are in dread as Creator and Lord, sing, ye priests; glorify, ye children; bless, ye peoples; and highly exalt Him unto all ages.[^1280]

*Troparion 1:* All the martyrs passed through the gapings of lions unharmed, and strongly outran the boilings of caldrons, being divinely refreshed by the fire of the Divine Spirit.[^1281]

*Troparion 2:* Thou didst grant unto Thy prophets to foresee things far off, and didst sacredly make wise Thy hierarchs; by their prayers, O Christ God, enlighten the hearts of those who faithfully sing unto Thee.[^1282]

*Troparion 3:* Having crucified yourselves unto the world, O Venerable ones, ye inherited otherworldly life, O hermits, with all who from the ages have been well-pleasing unto God in holiness and righteousness.[^1283]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 4:* O Thou Who didst fashion man from the earth in a manner befitting God, account the faithful whom Thou hast translated from the earth worthy of the delight of Paradise, as Thou art good, overlooking all things committed by them.[^1284]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Knowing thee clearly as fair and blameless among women, O pure Virgin, the women who suffered make supplications with thee unto God, that thy servants may be saved from tribulations.[^1285]

*Other Heirmos:* In the furnace the children of Israel, shining more purely than gold in the beauty of piety as in a crucible, said: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and highly exalt Him unto all ages.[^1286]

*Troparion 1:* Having justified by faith the faithful whom Thou hast translated, show them, O Savior, as standers at Thy right hand by the prayers of the passion-bearers, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and highly exalt Him unto all ages.[^1287]

*Troparion 2:* Having washed away all the defilement of Thy departed servants with the dew of Thy love for mankind, account them worthy to sing unto Thee in hymns: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and highly exalt Him unto all ages.[^1288]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having all power over death and life, vouchsafe that those who have fallen asleep in faith may attain Thy radiance, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and highly exalt Him unto all ages.[^1289]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou hast been the mediatress of salvation unto us, O All-immaculate One, and in...[^1290]

...never-passing ages of abode and brilliance; thee, O pure Virgin, do we, all the works, ever bless, and highly exalt unto all ages.[^1291]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* The life-receiving, ever-flowing fountain, the light-bearing lamp of grace, the animate temple, the all-pure tabernacle, more spacious than heaven and earth, the Theotokos, do we the faithful magnify.[^1292]

*Troparion 1:* Let us honor the sacred contests of the sacred martyrs, rejoicing in their sufferings, wounds, nailings, banishments, and blessed slaying, whereby they appeared as heirs of Christ.[^1293]

*Troparion 2:* As priests of God, the divine pleasers clothed themselves in righteousness; having lived in holiness, they that visibly finished their course in fasting rejoice, magnifying Christ.[^1294]

*Troparion 3:* Having become the divine mouths of the Lord, all the prophets proclaimed His light unto all; with whom now the women who struggled valiantly and pleased God by fasting also rejoice.[^1295]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 4:* O Power that reigns over all, show those whom Thou hast translated from the earth to be partakers of Thy kingdom with Thy saints; and overlook what hath been committed by them, O God of all, by Thine exceeding goodness.[^1296]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst bear the divine, timeless Light, loose all my temporal transgressions through thy prayers; and enlighten my mind which is ever darkened by slothfulness, that I may sing unto thee and faithfully magnify thee.[^1297]

*Other Heirmos:* The bush burning with fire yet unconsumed showed forth the image of thy pure childbearing; and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of temptations raging against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos.[^1298]

*Troparion 1:* Spare Thy creation, as Thou art a God Who art the Lover of mankind and merciful, and give rest in the mansions of Thy saints, where all the martyrs rejoice, unto those who in faith have been translated from temporal things, O Abundant in mercy.[^1299]

*Troparion 2:* O Thou Who hast an abyss of mercies overcoming the transgressions of Thy servants: receiving those whom Thou hast chosen, give them rest in the bosom of Abraham, O Lover of mankind, and settle them with Lazarus in Thy light.[^1300]

*Glory...*
*Troparion 3:* Having become the Redeemer and Savior of the human race by reason of the Crucifixion...[^1301]

...whom Thou hast now translated from us, as the Benefactor, account worthy of divine enjoyment, and of life incorruptible, and of joy and radiance.[^1302]

*Theotokion:*
O the marvels of thy childbearing, surpassing the mind! For thou alone, O Virgin, hast granted unto all those under the sun to understand the newest marvel, O All-pure One, of thine inscrutable birthgiving. Therefore do we all magnify thee.[^1303]

*Then: It is truly meet: and the rest.*[^1304]

*At the Praises.*
*Stichera to the Martyrs, Tone 1:*
Come, all ye people, let us honor the passion-bearers of Christ with spiritual songs and hymns, as the illuminators of the world and preachers of the faith, the ever-flowing fountains whence healings pour forth unto the faithful. By their prayers, O Christ our God, grant peace unto Thy world, and great mercy unto our souls.[^1305]

These soldiers of the great King resisted the commands of the tyrants, valiantly disregarding torments; and having trampled down all deceit, they were worthily crowned, and they ask of the Savior peace for our souls, and great mercy.[^1306]

Neither tribulation, nor distress, nor famine, nor persecution, nor wounds, nor the fury of wild beasts, nor the sword, nor threatening fire was able to separate you from God, O all-lauded martyrs; but rather through love toward Him, having struggled as in alien bodies, ye forgot nature and despised death. Therefore also ye have worthily received the reward of your labors, having become heirs of the heavenly kingdom; pray Christ for our souls.[^1307]

Rejoice in the Lord, O martyrs, for ye have fought the good fight; ye resisted kings, and conquered tyrants; ye feared not fire or sword, nor wild beasts devouring your bodies; sending up a hymn unto Christ with the angels, ye received heavenly crowns; entreat that peace may be granted unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls.[^1308]

*For the Departed:*
Showing forth in deed, O my Savior, that Thou art the resurrection of all, by a word, O Word, Thou didst raise Lazarus from the dead; and when the dead arose from the tombs...[^1309]

...and the gates of hades were confounded, then unto men death appeared as sleep. But O Thou Who camest for the salvation of Thine own creation and not for condemnation, give rest unto them whom Thou hast chosen, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^1310]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice from us, O holy Virgin Theotokos, pure vessel of the whole universe, unquenchable lamp, receptacle of the Uncontainable, undefiled temple! Rejoice, from whom was born the Lamb of God, Who takes away the sins of the whole world.[^1311]

*At the Aposticha.*
*Stichera for the Departed. A Work of Kyr Theophanes, Tone 1:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃ници):*
We pray Thee, O Savior: account those who have been translated worthy of Thy sweet communion, and settle them in the mansions of the righteous, with Thy saints, and in heavenly dwellings, overlooking iniquities through Thy tender compassion, and granting rest unto them.[^1312]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.*[^1313]
Beyond visible things are Thy promises, O Savior, which eye hath not seen, and ear hath not heard, and have never entered into the heart of man; therefore we pray Thee, O Master: account those who have been translated unto Thee worthy to obtain Thy sweet communion, and grant them eternal life.[^1314]

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*[^1315]
Rejoicing in Thy Cross, hoping in Thy Cross, Thy servants have passed over unto Thee, O Lover of mankind! Unto whom now grant Thy Cross as the deliverance of their transgressions, and the Blood poured out for the life of the world, by Thy tender mercy forgiving sins, and enlightening them with the light of Thy countenance.[^1316]

*Verse: And their memory is from generation to generation.*[^1317]
No one among men hath been sinless, no one, save Thou alone, O Immortal One. Therefore appoint Thy servants, as a merciful God, in the light with Thine angelic choirs, overlooking iniquities through Thy tender compassion, and granting them forgiveness.[^1318]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Entreat Christ, Thine Offspring, O Virgin Mother, to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto Thy servants who have in truth proclaimed thee as the Theotokos, and have blessed thee with pious words, and...[^1319]

...to account them worthy of the radiance and joy of the saints in His kingdom.[^1320]

*On Saturday, the Beatitudes, Tone 1:*
By food the enemy led Adam out of Paradise, but by the Cross Christ brought the thief into it, who cried aloud: Remember me, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom.[^1321]

An unnumbered multitude of sufferers, hierarchs, all-wise women, and most glorious prophets entreat Thee, O Jesus God: grant us remission of transgressions, and great mercy.[^1322]

Having finished your course, O most sacred sufferers of Christ, with the sacred hierarchs and prophets, ye were accounted worthy to dwell in the heavenly city, rejoicing with the angels.[^1323]

In the unwaning light, O Christ, appoint those whom Thou hast translated, overlooking their transgressions as a merciful God, that we may glorify Thy rich mercy, O Benefactor.[^1324]

*Glory...*
With the Father without beginning, and Thy Holy Spirit, O Christ God, we all glorify Thy love for mankind, crying aloud: Remember Thy servants in the hour of judgment, O Lord.[^1325]

*Both now... Theotokion:*
As a spacious palace, and throne of glory, and radiant cloud do we hymn thee, O Pure One, and we pray: dispel the dark cloud from our souls by thy divine prayers.[^1326]

*End of the First Tone.*[^1327]

---

### Notes

[^15]: Printed folio 1 opens Tone 1 on Saturday evening. The top engraving features six medallions portraying Old Testament types and New Testament hierarchs surrounding the Good Shepherd.
[^16]: Little Vespers (Малая вечерня) is the abbreviated vesperal office chanted in the late afternoon prior to the All-Night Vigil on Saturday and Great Feast days.
[^17]: At Little Vespers, the Psalter verses on "Lord, I have cried" are reduced to 4 (from the standard 10 of Great Vespers). The first Resurrectional sticheron is repeated to yield four hymns.
[^18]: Saint John Damascene (c. 676–749), Father and Doctor of the Church, traditionally revered as the primary compiler and hymnographer of the Byzantine Octoechos.
[^19]: Incipit: Ѡбиди́те лю́дїе Сїẃнъ (Greek: Κυκλώσατε λαοὶ Σιών). Cf. LXX Ps. 47:13.
[^20]: LXX Ps. 92:1: Ὁ Κύριος ἐβασίλευσεν, εὐπρέπειαν ἐνεδύσατο (The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty).
[^21]: LXX Ps. 44:18 (Masoretic 45:17): μνησθήσομαι τοῦ ὀνόματός σου ἐν πάσῃ γενεᾷ καὶ γενεᾷ.
[^22]: LXX Ps. 44:11 (Masoretic 45:10): ἄκουσον, θύγατερ, καὶ ἴδε καὶ κλῖνον τὸ οὖς σου.
[^23]: LXX Ps. 44:13 (Masoretic 45:12): τὸ πρόσωπόν σου λιτανεύσουσιν οἱ πλούσιοι τοῦ λαοῦ.
[^24]: LXX Ps. 71:6 (Masoretic 72:6): καταβήσεται ὡς ὑετὸς ἐπὶ πόκον.
[^25]: Physical Leaf p7 = Printed Folio 5.
[^26]: Great Vespers (Великая вечерня) chanted on Saturday evening as the opening office of the Sunday Resurrectional cycle.
[^27]: The first Kathisma of the Psalter ("Blessed is the man," Pss. 1–8) appointed for Saturday Great Vespers.
[^28]: The rubrical distribution allocates 3 Resurrectional stichera of the Octoechos, 4 Eastern stichera, and 3 from the Menaion (or 6 if a ranked saint is commemorated).
[^29]: Eastern Stichera (Восточны / Ἀνατολικά), ancient anonymous Resurrectional stichera preserved in the Octoechos, attributed by tradition to the Church of Antioch or Anatolius of Constantinople.
[^30]: Scriptural imagery drawing from LXX Isa. 44:23 and 49:13: Εὐφράνθητε, οὐρανοί... ῥηξάτωσαν τὰ ὄρη εὐφροσύνην.
[^31]: The Saturday Great Vespers Sunday Prokimenon in Tone 6: LXX Ps. 92:1.
[^32]: Verse 1 of the Sunday Prokimenon: LXX Ps. 92:1.
[^33]: Verse 2 of the Sunday Prokimenon: LXX Ps. 92:1.
[^34]: Verse 3 of the Sunday Prokimenon: LXX Ps. 92:5.
[^35]: Alphabetical Stichera (Стїхи̑ры по а҆лфави́ту / Στιχηρὰ κατ' ἀλφάβητον): A series of Aposticha stichera arranged in Greek acrostic order according to the letters of the alphabet (Α, Β, Γ...), here in Church Slavonic translation.
[^36]: LXX Isa. 7:14: ἰδοὺ ἡ παρθένος ἐν γαστρὶ ἕξει καὶ τέξεται υἱόν.
[^37]: LXX Ps. 131:8 (Masoretic 132:8): ἀνάστηθι, Κύριε, εἰς τὴν ἀνάπαυσίν σου, σὺ καὶ ἡ κιβωτὸς τοῦ ἁγιάσματός σου.
[^38]: Small Compline (Малое повечерие) appointed for Saturday evening after Great Vespers, featuring the Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.
[^39]: LXX Ps. 50 (Masoretic Ps. 51), the Great Penitential Psalm of David.
[^40]: LXX Ps. 69 (Masoretic Ps. 70), "O God, draw near unto my help."
[^41]: LXX Ps. 142 (Masoretic Ps. 143), the final penitential Psalm of the Six Psalms and Compline.
[^42]: The Nicene-Constantinopolitan Creed, recited at Compline and the Midnight Office.
[^43]: The Saturday Compline Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos in Tone 1, chanted during Small Compline.
[^44]: Biblical quotation combining LXX 1 Reg. 2:10 (1 Sam. 2:10) and Jer. 9:23: μὴ καυχάσθω ὁ σοφὸς ἐν τῇ σοφίᾳ αὐτοῦ, καὶ μὴ καυχάσθω ὁ ἰσχυρὸς ἐν τῇ ἰσχύι αὐτοῦ, καὶ μὴ καυχάσθω ὁ πλούσιος ἐν τῷ πλούτῳ αὐτοῦ.
[^45]: Typological reference to Jacob's Ladder: Gen. 28:12.
[^46]: Habakkuk's prophetic vision of the Theophany: LXX Hab. 3:2–3, 13: Ὁ Θεὸς ἐκ Θαιμὰν ἥξει, καὶ ὁ ἅγιος ἐξ ὄρους κατασκίου δασέος... ἐξῆλθες εἰς σωτηρίαν λαοῦ σου, τοῦ σῶσαι τὸν χριστόν σου.
[^47]: Canticle of Canticles (Song of Songs) 3:6, 8:5: Τίς αὕτη ἡ ἀναβαίνουσα ἀπὸ τῆς ἐρήμου;
[^48]: The Rod and Branch of Jesse: LXX Isa. 11:1.
[^49]: The Annunciation: Luke 1:35: Πνεῦμα ἅγιον ἐπελεύσεται ἐπὶ σέ, καὶ δύναμις Ὑψίστου ἐπισκιάσει σοι.
[^50]: Phil. 4:7: καὶ ἡ εἰρήνη τοῦ Θεοῦ ἡ ὑπερέχουσα πάντα νοῦν φρουρήσει τὰς καρδίας ὑμῶν.
[^51]: The Stone cut without hands from the mountain: Dan. 2:34, 45.
[^52]: The Prophet Jonah swallowed by the whale as a prefiguration of the three-day burial and resurrection of Christ: Jon. 2:1–7.
[^53]: The Cornerstone: LXX Ps. 117:22 (Masoretic 118:22); Isa. 28:16; Eph. 2:20.
[^54]: Marian types in the Tabernacle furnishings: Exod. 25:31 (the golden lampstand), Exod. 16:33 and Heb. 9:4 (the golden urn containing the manna).
[^55]: Aaron's rod that budded: Num. 17:8; Heb. 9:4.
[^56]: Sessional Hymn (Сѣдаленъ / Κάθισμα) chanted after Ode 6 of the Canon at Compline.
[^57]: The Three Holy Youths in the fiery furnace of Babylon: Dan. 3:19–25. Leaf p10 terminates mid-sentence at the end of the printed page; the remainder of Ode 7 continues on physical leaf p11 (printed fol. 9).
[^58]: LXX Dan. 3:52: Εὐλογητὸς εἶ, ὁ Θεὸς τῶν πατέρων ἡμῶν. Leaf p10 concluded mid-sentence; the completion of the Heirmos of Ode 7 is on physical leaf p11 (printed fol. 9).
[^59]: Ezekiel's prophecy of the closed eastern gate: LXX Ezek. 44:2.
[^60]: Allusion to the sevenfold heated Babylonian furnace: LXX Dan. 3:19.
[^61]: Dan. 3:24–27.
[^62]: LXX Dan. 3:57 (The Song of the Three Youths): Εὐλογεῖτε, πάντα τὰ ἔργα Κυρίου, τὸν Κύριον.
[^63]: The parable of the sower and the thorns: Matt. 13:7, 22; Mark 4:7; LXX Gen. 3:18.
[^64]: Aaron's rod that budded: Num. 17:8; Heb. 9:4.
[^65]: LXX Ps. 71:6: Καταβήσεται ὡς ὑετὸς ἐπὶ πόκον.
[^66]: LXX Ezek. 1:26, 10:1.
[^67]: 3 Kings (1 Kings) 8:27; 2 Chron. 6:18.
[^68]: LXX Ps. 87:5–7 (Masoretic 88:4–6).
[^69]: In the 1895 Lviv Stauropegion edition, printed under the Austro-Hungarian Dual Monarchy, `(и҆́мⷶ)` represents Emperor Franz Joseph I (1830–1916; reigned 1848–1916).
[^70]: The prayer of Paul the Monk of the Monastery of the Benefactress (Εὐεργέτιδος): Ἄσπιλε, ἀμόλυντε, ἄφθορε, ἄχραντε, ἁγνὴ Παρθένε.
[^71]: The Sunday Midnight Office (Последованіе Полунощницы недѣльныя) is served before Sunday Matins when an All-Night Vigil is not celebrated. It contains the Triadic Canon of the Tone by Metrophanes, replacing the usual weekday readings from Kathisma 17 and the Midnight prayers.
