[^241]: Beatitudes Troparion 3: Christ the Sinless One voluntarily crucified and buried, raising Adam with Himself (1 Cor. 15:22).
[^242]: Beatitudes Troparion 4: Christ raising the temple of His body by His three-day burial (John 2:19–21).
[^243]: Beatitudes Troparion 5: The Myrrh-bearing women at the sepulcher early in the morning and the angel in white robes (Mark 16:1–6). The stanza begins at the bottom of printed fol. 29 (physical leaf p31) and concludes on physical leaf p32.
[^244]: The Angel's proclamation to the Myrrh-bearers: "Why seek ye the living among the dead? He is risen" (Luke 24:5–6; Matt. 28:5–7).
[^245]: The Great Commission atop the mountain in Galilee: "Go ye therefore, and teach all nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost" (Matt. 28:16–20).
[^246]: Trinitarian triadikon of the Beatitudes in Tone 1, confessing the consubstantial Trinity.
[^247]: Theotokion of the Beatitudes: The Church pleading the maternal intercession of the Virgin Mary before the Risen Christ.
[^248]: Sunday Evening at Vespers opens the liturgical cycle of Monday, dedicated throughout the Byzantine rite to the Holy Bodiless Powers of Heaven (the Nine Ranks of Angels).
[^249]: Penitential Sticheron 1: Tone 1, Podoben "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льніи мꙋ́ченици / Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες). Luke 15:4–6 (the Good Shepherd carrying the lost sheep upon His shoulders) conflated with the return of the Prodigal Son (Luke 15:11–32).
[^250]: Penitential Sticheron 2: Creation ex nihilo and the formation of man in the divine image (Gen. 1:26–27; 2:7), restored through the Incarnation.
[^251]: Penitential Sticheron 3: Christ the true Son of the heavenly Father becoming the Son of Man to seek out humanity deceived by the serpent (Gen. 3:1–6; Luke 19:10).
[^252]: Stichera to the Bodiless Powers (Безпло́тнымъ / Ἀσωμάтоις). Sticheron 1: The immaterial celestial hierarchies standing before the divine throne and illuminated by the uncreated light (Dionysius the Areopagite, *De Coelesti Hierarchia*). The stanza begins at the bottom of printed fol. 30 (physical leaf p32) and continues on physical leaf p33.
[^253]: Sticheron 1 to the Bodiless Powers (concluded): The angels designated as "secondary lights" (φῶта δεύτερα) reflecting the primary divine light (cf. St. Gregory the Theologian, Oration 40.5).
[^254]: Sticheron 2 to the Bodiless Powers: Receiving imperishable life from the First Life and beholding eternal wisdom.
[^255]: Sticheron 3 to the Bodiless Powers: The nine angelic ranks explicitly invoked (Archangels, Angels, Principalities, Thrones, Dominions, six-winged Seraphim, many-eyed Cherubim, Powers, Authorities; cf. Pseudo-Dionysius).
[^256]: Theotokion at Lord, I have cried: Pleading the Virgin's intercession with the bodiless hosts before the evening of death arrives.
[^257]: The Sunday evening Prokeimenon, Tone 1: LXX Ps. 133:1–2 ("Behold now, bless ye the Lord, all ye servants of the Lord").
[^258]: Penitential Aposticha Sticheron 1: Sinking in the abyss of transgressions and begging for Christ's hand as Peter did on the sea (Matt. 14:28–31).
[^259]: LXX Ps. 122:1: "Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven."
[^260]: Penitential Aposticha Sticheron 2: Condemned by evil thoughts and deeds, praying for the gift of repentance.
[^261]: LXX Ps. 122:3: "Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us, for we are utterly filled with contempt."
[^262]: Aposticha Sticheron 3: Supplication through the prayers of all Saints and the Theotokos.
[^263]: Theotokion Automelon: Podoben "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ / Τῶν οὐρανίων таγμάτων).
[^264]: Concluding rubrics of Sunday Vespers: "Lord, now lettest Thou Thy servant depart" (Luke 2:29–32); Trisagion prayers; Troparion of the Saint of the day (from the Menaion); Glory... Both now... Theotokion; Litany and Dismissal.
[^265]: On Sunday at Compline (Въ Недѣ́лю на Повече́рїи): The Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos (Канѡ́нъ моле́бный прест҃ѣ́й Бцⷣѣ) in Tone 1.
[^266]: Ode 1 Heirmos: LXX Exod. 15:6 ("Thy right hand, O Lord, is glorified in strength; Thy right hand, O Lord, hath shattered the enemies").
[^267]: Refrain to the Theotokos: "Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us."
[^268]: Ode 1, Troparion 1: Ineffably conceiving God and beyond nature giving birth unto Him, O all-pure Sovereign Lady, Who redeemed earthly beings from transgressions.
[^269]: Ode 1, Troparion 2: Sinking in the abyss of sins, begging the Virgin's pilotage unto the calm harbor of repentance.
[^270]: Ode 1, Troparion 3: Pleading deliverance from impending torments.
[^271]: Ode 1, Troparion 4: Calming the assault of tribulations and storm of temptations through the Mother of the Redeemer.
[^272]: Ode 3 Heirmos: 1 Kings 2:2–4 (1 Sam. 2:2–4); Luke 24:49 ("Endued with power from on high"). Christ the Lover of mankind as the animated temple of glory.
[^273]: Ode 3, Troparion 1: The sight of the Virgin's pure image is sweet to Angels, saving to men, and dreadful to demons.
[^274]: Ode 3, Troparion 2: The Creator clothing Himself with flesh from virginal blood (Heb. 2:14).
[^275]: Ode 3, Troparion 3: The Theotokos as guardian in life and shelter at departure unto eternal life.
[^276]: Ode 3, Troparion 4: The seedless conception and incorrupt childbirth of God incarnate. The stanza begins at the bottom of printed fol. 32 (physical leaf p34) and concludes on physical leaf p35.
[^277]: Ode 3, Troparion 4 (concluded): Calling human nature back from its fall.
[^278]: Ode 4 Heirmos: LXX Hab. 3:2–3 ("God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from the dark, overshadowing mount"). The mountain overshadowed by divine grace.
[^279]: Ode 4, Troparion 1: The unrestrained inclination toward evil and the assault of the adversary before repentance.
[^280]: Ode 4, Troparion 2: The demonic practitioners of wicked deeds seeking to drag the soul away (Matt. 18:7–9).
[^281]: Ode 4, Troparion 3: Groaning from the depth of the soul for remission of debts contracted through foolishness (Matt. 6:12).
[^282]: Ode 4, Troparion 4: The cunning enemy attempting to swallow the soul through grievous memories and thoughts (1 Pet. 5:8).
[^283]: Ode 5 Heirmos: LXX Isa. 9:2; 26:9. Christ enlightening the ends of the world with the radiance of His Coming and Cross.
[^284]: Ode 5, Troparion 1: Pleading for deliverance from the arrows of the stranger (Eph. 6:16) and the future trial.
[^285]: Ode 5, Troparion 2: Marian invocation as strength, praise, rejoicing, guardian, refuge, and invincible champion.
[^286]: Ode 5, Troparion 3: Cleansing the defiled soul through the sprinkling of divine prayer and bestowing the radiant garment of salvation (Rev. 7:14; 19:8). The stanza begins at the bottom of printed fol. 33 (physical leaf p35) and concludes on physical leaf p36.
[^287]: Ode 5, Theotokion (concluded): The Virgin as steady pilot across tempestuous tribulations and intercessor at the soul's departure (cf. Prayers at the Departure of the Soul).
[^288]: Ode 6 Heirmos: LXX Ps. 43:23 (Masoretic 44:22); Rom. 8:36 ("Accounted as sheep for the slaughter"); Jonah 2:3, 6.
[^289]: Ode 6, Troparion 1: Pleading for the Virgin's outstretched hand from the abyss of evils.
[^290]: Ode 6, Troparion 2: Arising to the defense of her servant lest the enemy seize and destroy him.
[^291]: Ode 6, Troparion 3: Healer of the sick, resurrection of the fallen, cleansing of sinners.
[^292]: Ode 6, Troparion 4: Rescuing the humble soul from the abyss prepared by demonic sloth.
[^293]: Sessional Hymn at Compline: Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се / Τὸν τάφον σου Σωτήρ). The faithful fleeing to the Virgin's holy temple for deliverance from demonic malice and fearful judgment.
[^294]: Ode 7 Heirmos: LXX Dan. 3:19–27. The Theotokos as the spiritual/noetic furnace (νοητὴ κάμινος) renewing the world as the God of our fathers preserved the Three Children.
[^295]: Ode 7, Troparion 1: The divine tabernacle (σκηνή) of the Master, delivering from passions, sicknesses, sorrows, and debts.
[^296]: Ode 7, Troparion 2: Quelling intolerable sickness and the turbid waves of transgressions. The stanza begins at the bottom of printed fol. 34 (physical leaf p36) and concludes on physical leaf p37.
[^297]: Ode 7, Troparion 2 (concluded): True joy granted and turbid waves of transgression dispelled.
[^298]: Ode 7, Troparion 3: The Theotokos as invincible pillar, foundation, and surety of attaining the heavenly Kingdom (1 Tim. 3:15).
[^299]: Ode 7, Troparion 4: The Orient/Dawn of the Sun of glory delivering from outer darkness (Matt. 8:12; 22:13; Luke 1:78).
[^300]: Ode 8 Heirmos: LXX Dan. 3:57–88; Song of the Three Holy Children 35–65 ("Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing praise and exalt Him supremely unto all ages").
[^301]: Ode 8, Troparion 1: Bringing forth the Author and Giver of peace (Isa. 9:6) to quell turbulent passions.
[^302]: Ode 8, Troparion 2: The Mediatress of salvation granting true song throughout all ages.
[^303]: Ode 8, Troparion 3: Ineffably conceiving the Creator of all and God, delivering from corruption and temptation.
[^304]: Ode 8, Troparion 4: Pleading the Creator for cleansing of sins, deliverance from sorrows, and release from eternal fire.
[^305]: Ode 9 Heirmos: LXX Exod. 3:2; Dan. 3:19–27. The unburnt bush prefiguring virgin childbirth; beseeching the quenching of the furnace of raging temptations.
[^306]: Ode 9, Troparion 1: Begging the Virgin's compassionate heart and deliverance from the jaws of the spiritual wolf (John 10:12; 1 Pet. 5:8). The stanza begins at the bottom of printed fol. 35 (physical leaf p37) and concludes on physical leaf p38.
[^307]: Ode 9, Troparion 1 (concluded): Beseeching mercy and that the servant may not be turned away ashamed (LXX Ps. 118:116).
[^308]: Ode 9, Troparion 2: Deliverance from many misfortunes, griefs, the wagging tongues of enemies, and eternal fire.
[^309]: Ode 9, Troparion 3: Sole visitation of the sick, restoration of the fallen, and guide unto God.
[^310]: Ode 9, Troparion 4: Pleading for remission of sins in the face of overwhelming despair.
[^311]: Concluding rubrics of Small Compline: "It is truly meet to bless thee, O Theotokos" (Megalynarion); Trisagion prayers, Troparion, and Dismissal.
[^312]: Monday Morning at Matins (Въ Понедѣ́лникъ ᲂу҆́тра): Penitential Sessional Hymns after the 1st Kathisma of the Psalter, Tone 1.

[^316]: 1st Kathisma, Theotokion: The wonder of wonders, conceiving without seed Him Whom angelic ranks cannot gaze upon (1 Pet. 1:12).

[^320]: LXX Ps. 67:36 (Masoretic 68:35): "Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel."

[^322]: 2nd Kathisma, Theotokion: The steadfast protectress and unshakeable hope of the world.
[^323]: Sessional Hymns when a 3rd Kathisma is chanted (as appointed by the Typikon during certain liturgical seasons): Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се / Τὸν τάφον σου Σωτήρ). The dreadful judgment seat and standing on the right hand of Christ (Matt. 25:31–33).

[^345]: Ode 3, Martyrikon 2: Theological confession of the Trinity in Persons and Unity in Essence, dispelling the darkness of polytheism.

[^412]: Monday Matins Aposticha: Sticheron 1 (Penitential): Warning the soul of the forthcoming judgment and urging immediate repentance.

[^416]: Aposticha Psalm Verse 2: LXX Ps. 89:17.
[^417]: Aposticha Martyrikon: Universal veneration of the passion-bearers as lamps of faith and fountains of miraculous healing.
[^418]: Aposticha Theotokion: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the celestial ranks" (Нб҃ныхъ чинѡ́въ / Τῶν οὐρανίων ταγμάτων). The Mother of God holier than all celestial powers.
[^419]: Concluding rubrics of Monday Matins: Trisagion prayers, Troparion of the Day/Saint, Litany, and Dismissal followed by the First Hour.
[^420]: Monday Typika (Beatitudes): Antithesis between Adam's expulsion through food and the good thief's entrance into Paradise through the Cross (Luke 23:42).
[^421]: Beatitudes, Troparion 1: Seeking a fountain of compunction to cleanse boundless evil deeds.
[^422]: Beatitudes, Troparion 2: Pleading the intercession of the angelic ranks for the pardon of voluntary and involuntary sins.
[^423]: Beatitudes, Martyrikon: The martyrs overwhelming the spiritual Pharaoh in streams of their sacrificial blood.
[^424]: Beatitudes, Triadikon: Doxology to the Holy Trinity: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.
[^425]: Beatitudes, Theotokion: The Mother of the Timeless Light guiding the soul out of demonic gloom and saving from future fire.
[^426]: Opening of Tuesday: The Holy Forerunner and Baptist John. In the Byzantine rite, the liturgical day of Tuesday commences at Vespers on Monday evening.
[^427]: Monday Evening Vespers: Lord, I Have Cried, Penitential Sticheron 1 (Joseph): Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льніи мꙋ́ченици / Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες). Repentance of the prodigal son (Luke 15:13, 18).
[^428]: Penitential Sticheron 2: The kenosis of Christ enriching mankind (2 Cor. 8:9); escaping the torment of the rich man and receiving the portion of poor Lazarus (Luke 16:19–31).
[^429]: Penitential Sticheron 3: Lamenting the neglect of the spiritual faculties while indulging the passions; crying for mercy at the gates of divine judgment.
[^430]: Stichera to the Holy Forerunner: Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льніи мꙋ́ченици / Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες). Supplicating the Forerunner for streams of tears to heal a withered heart.
[^431]: Sticheron 2 to the Forerunner: John the Baptist baptizing the Lamb of God Who takes away the sins of the world (John 1:29).
[^432]: Sticheron 3 to the Forerunner: The Baptist as the herald of repentance (Matt. 3:1–2) enlightening nations.
[^433]: Vespers Theotokion: Beseeching the Virgin and the Forerunner to intercede with the Lamb of God, separating the soul from the goats and placing it among the sheep (Matt. 25:32–33).
[^434]: Evening Prokeimenon: LXX Ps. 4:4b, 2a.
[^435]: Monday Evening Vespers: Evening litany and the ancient evening prayer "Vouchsafe, O Lord, in this evening to keep us without sin."
[^436]: Monday Evening Aposticha: Sticheron 1 (Penitential): Sinking in the deep sea of transgressions and stretching forth the hand like the Apostle Peter (Matt. 14:30–31).
[^437]: Aposticha Psalm Verse 1: LXX Ps. 122:1–2.
[^438]: Aposticha Sticheron 2 (Penitential): Entreating the spirit of repentance for wicked thoughts and sinful deeds.
[^439]: Aposticha Psalm Verse 2: LXX Ps. 122:3–4.
[^440]: Aposticha Martyrikon: The martyrs confessing Christ before the tribunal, despising earthly life, and offering their severed heads as a sweet-smelling sacrifice.
[^441]: Aposticha Theotokion: Typological vision of Moses seeing the unburnt bush on Mount Horeb (Exod. 3:2) prefiguring the virgin birth.
[^442]: Concluding rubrics of Monday Evening Vespers: Canticle of Simeon (Luke 2:29–32), Trisagion prayers, Troparion, and Dismissal.
[^443]: Monday Compline: Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 1. Ode 1 Heirmos: LXX Exod. 15:1. "Let us all sing a triumphant song unto God, Who hath wrought marvelous wonders..."
[^444]: Marian Refrain for the Compline Canon: "Most holy Lady Theotokos, save us."
[^445]: Compline Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1: Praising the Mother of God as sanctuary, angelic ornament, and vessel of the Holy Spirit.
[^446]: Ode 1, Troparion 2: The Virgin as Queen and daughter of the King (LXX Ps. 44:10) reigning eternally with Christ God.
[^447]: Trinitarian Doxology marker closing the printed column on folio 48 (physical leaf p50). The canon continues with the final troparion and Ode 3 on physical leaf p51.
[^448]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 3 (physical leaf p51, printed fol. 49). David the prophetic psalmist (LXX Ps. 131:11) and Isaiah (Isa. 7:14) extolling the virginal womb and birth-giving of the Mother of God.
[^449]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 1, Both now... Theotokion. Explicit iconodule dogmatic defense in Monday Compline canon: 'for it venerates the image of the incarnation of thy Son' (ѡ҆бразѡ́мъ бо воплоще́нїѧ Сн҃а твоегѡ̀ покланѧ́етсѧ), establishing the theological legitimacy of icon veneration rooted in the reality of the Incarnation.
[^450]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 3, Heirmos. Scriptural citation of LXX Psalm 117:22 ('The stone which the builders rejected, the same is become the head of the corner') and Isaiah 28:16, applied christologically in Matthew 21:42 and 1 Peter 2:6–7.
[^451]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 1. Affirmation of the perpetual virginity of Mary (*virginitas post partum*) through the grace of the Holy Spirit.
[^452]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 2. The reversal of the primordial curse upon womankind (Genesis 3:16) through the incarnation of the Son of God from the Virgin Mary.
[^453]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 3, Glory... Troparion 3. Patristic marine metaphor of the Theotokos as the 'haven of salvation' (приста́нище спасе́нїа / λιμὴν σωτηρίας) calming the tempest of sin.
[^454]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 3, Both now... Theotokion. Reiteration of iconophile theology: the Church venerates the painted likenesses of Christ, the Timeless Word Who entered historical time as One of the Holy Trinity.
[^455]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 4, Heirmos. Scriptural prophecy of Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX: 'In the midst of the two living creatures Thou shalt be known... when the time is come, Thou shalt be shown forth').
[^456]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 1. 'The seven-light mirror of the divine Prophets' (седмосвѣ́тлое зерца́ло бж҃твенныхъ Проро́къ), alluding to the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit (Isaiah 11:2) reflected in prophetic typology.
[^457]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 2. The proper angelic greeting of the Archangel Gabriel (Luke 1:28: 'Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee') as the fulfillment of the Law and Prophets.
[^458]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 3 (physical leaf p52, printed fol. 50). Typological exposition of Habakkuk 3:3 (the 'mountain overshadowed' interpreted as the Virgin Mary).
[^459]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 4, Both now... Theotokion. Epigraphic affirmation of Orthodox veneration of the images of Christ's incarnation against iconoclastic rejection.
[^460]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 5, Heirmos. Allusion to Isaiah 26:9 ('From the night my spirit wakes early unto Thee, O God') and 2 Corinthians 8:9 (Christ becoming poor for our salvation).
[^461]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 1. Typology of the Burning Bush (Exodus 3:2), wherein the material thorns burned with uncreated fire yet were not consumed, prefiguring the womb of the Virgin containing the Godhead without injury.
[^462]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 2. 'A blossoming spiritual gift' (слове́сное прино́симъ цвѣтꙋ́щее дарова́нїе), poetic terminology for rational, unbloody hymnody (*logikē latreia*, Romans 12:1).
[^463]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 5, Glory... Troparion 3. Classical Byzantine penitential modesty: defiled earthly lips daring not to render adequate praise unto the Mother of God.
[^464]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 5, Both now... Theotokion. Chalcedonian dogmatic definition: Christ is One Person in two natures or essences (є҆ди́нъ є҆́сть въ двою̀ сꙋществꙋ̀ / εἷς ἐν δύο οὐσίαις), truly God and truly man.
[^465]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 6, Heirmos. Scriptural type of the Prophet Jonah crying from the belly of Sheol (Jonah 2:2–7).
[^466]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 1. Marian typology of the golden seven-branched menorah (Exodus 25:31–37; Zechariah 4:2), bearing the spiritual lamp of the Holy Spirit.
[^467]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 2. The Theotokos as the life-bearing Tree of Life (Genesis 2:9; Revelation 22:2), whose Fruit slays the venomous primordial serpent and grants immortality to mankind.
[^468]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 6, Glory... Troparion 3. Contrite supplication that the Mother of God receive sincere intention despite the hymnographer's unworthiness.
[^469]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 6, Both now... Theotokion. Direct historical witness to the triumph of Orthodoxy over iconoclasm: 'The lawless rage in the tumult of bitter unbelief, whenever they merely see the assemblies of the Orthodox kissing thine image, O Virgin.'
[^470]: Monday Compline Sessional Hymn after Ode 6, Tone 1. Melodic Model (Podoben: 'Thy tomb, O Savior' / Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се). Prayer to the Virgin as the pilot (*kybernētis*) of human life.
[^471]: Monday Compline Sessional Hymn continuation (physical leaf p53, printed fol. 51). Traditional Ruthenian and Byzantine eschatological doctrine of the soul's transition at death, seeking protection from the accusations of demonic toll-gatherers (*telōnia*).
[^472]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 7, Heirmos. Scriptural canticle of the Three Holy Children in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:24–50).
[^473]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 1. Mystical bridal imagery of the Virgin Mother of God, whom the Lord as divine Lover chose for Himself.
[^474]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 2. Typology of Aaron's rod that miraculously blossomed without moisture (Numbers 17:8), prefiguring the virginal conception of the Divine Word.
[^475]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 7, Glory... Troparion 3. Ascetic tripartite schema: defilement of the bodily members, the rational faculties (word), and the spiritual soul.
[^476]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 7, Both now... Theotokion. High Christological confession: the hypostatic union of divinity and humanity in Christ, the pre-eternal God Who became unchangeable man, perfect in both natures.
[^477]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 8, Heirmos. The Song of the Three Holy Children (Daniel 3:57–88 / Benedicite).
[^478]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 1. 'The divine hierurgy of God' (Бж҃е́ственное сщ҃е́нїе / θεία ἱερουργία), denoting the entire salvific economy of the Incarnation.
[^479]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 2. The universal Christian invocation of the Mother of God as the unfailing hope of human souls.
[^480]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 8, Glory... Troparion 3. Cosmic victory over Satan and demonic despair through the birth of Christ from Mary.
[^481]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 8, Both now... Theotokion. Classical Seventh Ecumenical Council (Nicaea II, 787) iconodule thesis: the divine essence remains uncircumscribable and invisible, but the incarnate bodily form of Christ is truly depictable and worthy of veneration.
[^482]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 9, Heirmos (physical leaf p54, printed fol. 52). Typology of the luminous cloud and Gideon's fleece (Judges 6:36–40; LXX Psalm 71:6).
[^483]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 1. Chairetismoi/Salutations to the Virgin Mary, echoing the Archangel Gabriel's greeting and invoking her as refuge for sinners on earth.
[^484]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 2. Commemoration of the holy Righteous Ancestors of God Joachim and Anna, rejoicing over the birth of their blessed daughter Mary.
[^485]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 9, Glory... Troparion 3. Universal efficacy of the Nativity of the Virgin for the salvation of sinners and the humble.
[^486]: Monday Compline Canon, Ode 9, Both now... Theotokion. Dogmatic conclusion of the Compline Canon: confessing Christ in two natures (*en dyo physesin*) and in one Hypostasis (*mia hypostasei*).
[^487]: Tuesday Matins Opening (physical leaf p54, printed fol. 52). Printed headpiece marking the Tuesday morning cycle of the Octoechos, dedicated to the Holy Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John.
[^488]: Tuesday Matins, After the first stichology, Sessional Hymn 1, Tone 1. Allusions to LXX Psalm 50:7 ('For behold, I was conceived in iniquities') and the contrite prayer of the Publican (Luke 18:13).
[^489]: Tuesday Matins, After the first stichology, Sessional Hymn 2, Tone 1. Scriptural synthesis of 1 Peter 4:18 ('And if the righteous scarcely be saved, where shall the ungodly and the sinner appear?') and the Parable of the Laborers hired at the eleventh hour (Matthew 20:1–16).
[^490]: Tuesday Matins, After the first stichology, Theotokion, Tone 1. The Virgin Mary as the 'unbreakable wall' (неразрꙋши́маѧ стѣна̀ / τεῖχος ἀκαтаμάχητον) interceding against divine wrath and eternal death.
[^491]: Tuesday Matins, After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn 1, Tone 1 (physical leaf p55, printed fol. 53). Liturgical paraphrase of the Parable of the Prodigal Son (Luke 15:11–32), pleading for the opening of the Father's embracing arms.
[^492]: Tuesday Matins, After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn 2, Tone 1. Patristic moral typology of Job (Job 1:1–22; 2:1–10): the devil destroyed Job's bodily possessions and health, but could not rob his spiritual fortress of patience.
[^493]: Tuesday Matins, After the second stichology, Sessional Hymn for the Martyrs, Tone 1. Martial imagery of Christian martyrdom (2 Timothy 4:7: 'I have finished my course, I have kept the faith').
[^494]: Tuesday Matins, After the second stichology, Theotokion, Tone 1. The repentance of the city of Nineveh under the preaching of the Prophet Jonah (Jonah 3:4–10) averting divine destruction.
[^495]: Tuesday Matins, After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn 1, Tone 1. Melodic Model (Podoben: 'Thy tomb, O Savior' / Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се). Allegory of the Good Samaritan (Luke 10:30–37), with the soul wounded by the spiritual thieves of sin.
[^496]: Tuesday Matins, After the third stichology, Sessional Hymn 2 to the Forerunner, Tone 1. St. John the Baptist baptizing Christ in the Jordan River (Matthew 3:13–17; Mark 1:9–11).
[^497]: Tuesday Matins, After the third stichology, Theotokion, Tone 1. The leaping of John the Baptist in the womb of his mother Elizabeth during the Visitation (Luke 1:41–44).
[^498]: Tuesday Matins Canons Opening (physical leaf p56, printed fol. 54). First Canon of Compunction, composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer (c. 816–886), bearing the acrostic: 'Receive, O Christ, the supplication of my words' (Прїимѝ Хрⷭ҇тѐ моле́нїе мои́хъ слове́съ).
[^499]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Heirmos. The Song of Moses at the Red Sea (Exodus 15:1–19: 'The Lord is my strength and song, and He is become my salvation').
[^500]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1. Spiritual servitude under the passions of sin, seeking deliverance from the Lord.
[^501]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 2. Allegory of the spear of sin mortally wounding the soul, and appeal to Christ the Resurrection of the dead.
[^502]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 3 (For the Martyrs). The martyrs sanctifying Christ in their bodily torments (1 Peter 3:15).
[^503]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 4 (For the Martyrs). The martyrs as pillars and ramparts of the Church unshaken by demonic assaults.
[^504]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 1, Theotokion. The Theotokos as the fiery throne (Daniel 7:9) and animate bridal chamber of the Incarnate King.
[^505]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Holy Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John. Acrostic: 'Hear the voice of those who cry unto thee, O Blessed One' (Гла́съ оу҆слы́ши вопїю́щихъ тебѣ̀ Блаже́нне).
[^506]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1. St. John the Baptist as the prophetic 'voice of one crying in the wilderness' (Isaiah 40:3; Matthew 3:3; John 1:23) announcing the Word.
[^507]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 2. The Baptist as the morning star and dawn preceding the Sun of Righteousness (Malachi 4:2).
[^508]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 3. John the Baptist's preaching unto the dead in Hades, foretelling the descent of Christ the Life.
[^509]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 1, Theotokion. The Virgin Mary giving birth to the pre-eternal Son entering time.
[^510]: Tuesday Matins, Ode 3, Heirmos (physical leaf p57, printed fol. 55). Scriptural typology of the foundation cornerstone (LXX Psalm 117:22; 1 Corinthians 3:11).
[^511]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 1. Christ condescending to assume human infirmity to restore the divine image in fallen mankind.
[^512]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 2. The prodigal soul lamenting irrational transgression of God's life-giving commandments.
[^513]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 3 (For the Martyrs). Angelic amazement at the bodily fortitude of the holy martyrs conquering incorporeal demonic adversaries.
[^514]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 4 (For the Martyrs). The holy passion-bearers standing before the King of glory dyed in the crimson of their own blood.
[^515]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 3, Theotokion. Paradoxical childbearing of the Virgin: nursing the very Provider of all creation without knowing man.
[^516]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 1. The miraculous birth of John from the aged and barren Elizabeth (Luke 1:7, 36) prefiguring spiritual fruitfulness.
[^517]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 2. Supplication to be strengthened by the heavenly Bread of life (John 6:35, 51).
[^518]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 3. St. John pointing out the Lamb of God Who takes away the sin of the world (John 1:29).
[^519]: Codicological note: Physical leaf 58 (printed fol. 56) exhibits a printer's running header typographical error: 'Въ Вто́рникъ ве́чера' ('On Tuesday Evening') is printed instead of 'Въ Вто́рникъ оу҆́тра' ('On Tuesday at Matins'), as the text continues uninterrupted within Tuesday Matins Canons.
[^520]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 3, Theotokion (physical leaf p58, printed fol. 56). Patristic soteriological axiom (*quod non est assumptum non est sanatum*): the Word assumed whole Adam in order to redeem whole mankind.
[^521]: Tuesday Matins, Ode 4, Heirmos. Prophecy of Habakkuk 3:2.
[^522]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 1. The prodigal squandering of spiritual wealth and pleading for the restoration of the 'first robe' of baptismal purity (Luke 15:22).
[^523]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 2. Christ as the resurrection of the fallen, establishing the unstable heart upon the rock of true repentance.
[^524]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 3 (For the Martyrs). The martyrs imitating Christ crucified, their blood quenching the dark clouds of polytheism.
[^525]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 4 (For the Martyrs). Navigating the stormy waves of worldly torment dryshod into the haven of the kingdom.
[^526]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 4, Theotokion. The mystery of the invisible God becoming visible and tangible through the Virgin Mary.
[^527]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 1. The ascetic camel's hair garment of the Baptist (Matthew 3:4) contrasted with the soul's nakedness of virtue.
[^528]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 2. John baptizing Jesus, the 'Torrent of delight' (LXX Psalm 35:9: 'Thou shalt make them drink of the torrent of Thy pleasure').
[^529]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 4, Troparion 3 (physical leaf p59, printed fol. 57). Allusion to Isaiah 63:9 (LXX: 'Not an ambassador, nor an angel, but He Himself saved them').
[^530]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 4, Theotokion. The Mother of God as the sacred temple of the Holy Spirit (1 Corinthians 6:19).
[^531]: Tuesday Matins, Ode 5, Heirmos. Scriptural citation of Isaiah 26:12 ('Lord, Thou wilt grant us peace') and Romans 14:9 (Christ Lord of the dead and living).
[^532]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 1. Direct penitential petition from LXX Psalm 50:13 ('Cast me not away from Thy presence, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me').
[^533]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 2. The example of the contrite Publican smiting his breast (Luke 18:13).
[^534]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 3 (For the Martyrs). The holy martyrs as unshakeable spiritual luminaries dispelling demonic darkness.
[^535]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 4 (For the Martyrs). The martyrs as living stones built upon Christ the chief Cornerstone (1 Peter 2:4–5).
[^536]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 5, Theotokion. Dogmatic formula of the Sixth Ecumenical Council (Constantinople III, 680–681): Christ operating in two natural wills and operations, in one divine Hypostasis.
[^537]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 1. St. John the Baptist dwelling in the wilderness in the spirit and power of the Prophet Elijah (1 Kings 17:2–6; Luke 1:17).
[^538]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 2. The Baptist as an unyielding tower against the stratagems of the enemy.
[^539]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 5, Troparion 3. Proclamation of the light of Christ to those dwelling in darkness and the shadow of death (Isaiah 9:2; Matthew 4:16).
[^540]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 5, Theotokion (physical leaf p60, printed fol. 58). Davidic prophecy of the Queen standing at the right hand of the King (LXX Psalm 44:10).
[^541]: Tuesday Matins, Ode 6, Heirmos. The prayer of Jonah from the belly of the sea monster (Jonah 2:2–7).
[^542]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 1. Allusion to LXX Psalm 50:10 ('The bones that have been humbled shall rejoice'), lamenting the crushing of spiritual strength under self-indulgence.
[^543]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 2. Pleading for Christ's outstretched hand in the stormy deep, recalling Peter sinking in the sea (Matthew 14:30–31).
[^544]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 3 (For the Martyrs). The myriads of martyrs joining the myriads of heavenly hosts (Hebrews 12:22).
[^545]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 4 (For the Martyrs). Sufferers drinking from the river of spiritual delights (LXX Psalm 35:9).
[^546]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 6, Theotokion. The Theotokos as the 'tabernacle of consecration' (Сщ҃е́нїа сѣ́нь / σκηνὴ τοῦ ἁγιάσματος), prefigured by the Old Testament Tabernacle.
[^547]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 1. The Baptist's preaching of repentance (Matthew 3:2: 'Repent ye, for the kingdom of heaven is at hand').
[^548]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 2. Poetic paradox: St. John immersing the uncontainable divine Abyss into the Jordan river waters.
[^549]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 6, Troparion 3. Comprehensive petition for deliverance from spiritual, bodily, and worldly trials.
[^550]: Tuesday Matins, Forerunner Canon, Ode 6, Theotokion. The Virgin Mary 'blessed among women' (Luke 1:28, 42) invoked as guardian against every spiritual harm.
[^551]: Tuesday Matins, Ode 7, Heirmos. The Three Holy Children delivered unhurt from the furnace of fire (Daniel 3:49–50).
[^552]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 1 (physical leaf p61, printed fol. 59). Exemplum of the Patriarch Job (Job 1:21–22; 2:10; James 5:11), enduring demonic afflictions as an immovable spiritual pillar.
[^553]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Troparion 2. The rational soul degraded to irrationality through bodily lusts; invocation of Christ the Divine Word (Logos) Who saved the sinful woman with a word (Luke 7:37–50).
[^554]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, For the Martyrs, Troparion 3. The Holy Martyrs shedding their blood in imitation of the physical Passion of Christ, thereby reigning eternally with Him (Romans 8:17; 2 Timothy 2:12).
[^555]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, For the Martyrs, Troparion 4. Sacred martyrdom as the spiritual construction of a divine palace wherein Christ the King takes His rest.
[^556]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 7, Theotokion. Christ choosing Mary out of all generations as an honorable and spotless dwelling place, shining forth from her as the spiritual Sun to enlighten the world.
[^557]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7, Troparion 1. St. John the Baptist as the preeminent mediator between the Old Covenant and the New (Matthew 11:13; Luke 16:16). The Church Slavonic text displays an orthographic ligature/variant 'ѡ҆кнови́ти' for 'ѡ҆бнови́ти' (renew).
[^558]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7, Troparion 2. St. John establishing the rule and preaching of repentance upon earth (Matthew 3:1–2).
[^559]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7, Troparion 3. Troparion on the narrow way of ascetic self-mastery (Matthew 7:14). The 1895 Lviv edition contains a minor typographical slip: 'шигото́ю' for 'широто́ю' (by the breadth).
[^560]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7, Triadikon. Orthodox Trinitarian dogma: one divine essence (*ousia*), unoriginate Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, one reigning Sovereignty and Life-giving Principle.
[^561]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 7, Theotokion. Supplication to the Theotokos to intercede before God for the remission of sins and deliverance from eternal punishment.
[^562]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Heirmos (physical leaf p62, printed fol. 60). Canticle of the Three Holy Children (Daniel 3:57–88 / Benedicite), calling upon priests, children, and all creation to bless and supremely exalt the Lord.
[^563]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 1. The primordial deception of the serpent in Eden stripping man of virtues (Genesis 3:1–7), and Christ clothing the repentant soul in the robe of righteousness.
[^564]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 2. Eschatological expectation of the Dread Judgment (Matthew 25:31–46), praying for deliverance from the fire of Gehenna.
[^565]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, For the Martyrs, Troparion 3. Direct allusion to Romans 8:35–39: neither tribulation, distress, sword, nor fire being able to separate the martyrs from the love of Christ.
[^566]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, For the Martyrs, Troparion 4. Bodily martyrs wrestling against the incorporeal adversary (Ephesians 6:12) and now dancing in chorus with the incorporeal hosts of heaven.
[^567]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 8, Theotokion. Marian botanical imagery: the Virgin as the mystical lily or flower in the valleys (Song of Songs 2:1–2), fragrant with virtues and purity.
[^568]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8, Heirmos. Typological celebration of the miracle of the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:49–50), where the blazing flame was transformed into a cooling dew.
[^569]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8, Troparion 1. Classical Byzantine penitential self-accusation: acknowledging oneself as the chief of sinners and seeking salvation through the prayers of the Forerunner.
[^570]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8, Troparion 2. St. John the Baptist possessing angelic conduct and preaching the Angel of Great Counsel (Isaiah 9:6 LXX) unto the ends of the earth.
[^571]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8, Troparion 3. The unjust beheading of John the Baptist by Herod (Matthew 14:1–12), who had immersed the sacred head of Christ in the waters of the Jordan.
[^572]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 8, Theotokion. Scriptural imagery of Gehenna: the unquenchable flame, outer darkness, gnashing of teeth, and the undying worm (Mark 9:43–48; Matthew 8:12).
[^573]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Heirmos (physical leaf p63, printed fol. 61). Typological canticle of the luminous cloud and Gideon's fleece (Judges 6:36–40; LXX Ps. 71:6), magnifying the Virgin Mother of God.
[^574]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 1. Patristic ascetic exhortation on spiritual vigilance: awakening from the heavy slumber of despondency (*akēdia*) to engage in virtuous works of repentance (Romans 13:11–12).
[^575]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 2. Liturgical catalogue of Scriptural penitents: the Publican (Luke 18:13), the sinful woman weeping at Jesus' feet (Luke 7:38), the Wise Thief (Luke 23:42), the Prodigal Son (Luke 15:18), and the Canaanite woman (Matthew 15:22).
[^576]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, For the Martyrs, Troparion 3. The Holy Passion-bearers as spiritual physicians healing bodily passions and spiritual brokenness through their courageous martyrdom.
[^577]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, For the Martyrs, Troparion 4. The radiant endurance of the martyrs outshining the sun, defeating the prince of darkness, and scattering the night of idolatry.
[^578]: Tuesday Matins, Penitential Canon, Ode 9, Theotokion. Prayer to the Mother of God to illumine the soul obscured by the dark soot of worldly pleasures through the spiritual radiance of the Incarnate Word.
[^579]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Troparion 1. Poetic allegories of St. John the Baptist: a shining lamp unto those in darkness, and a melodious swallow proclaiming the spiritual spring of salvation.
[^580]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Troparion 2. St. John invoked as the friend of the Bridegroom (John 3:29), interceding to rekindle the lamp of the soul extinguished by spiritual sloth (Matthew 25:1–13).
[^581]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Troparion 3. St. John the Baptist interceding alongside the bodiless angels, the holy martyrs, and the prophets. The text transitions across leaves p63–p64.
[^582]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Troparion 4 (physical leaf p64, printed fol. 62). Ascetic panegyric to St. John: fair swallow, noble nightingale, dove, and wilderness-loving turtledove (Song of Songs 2:12), beseeching fruitfulness in virtues.
[^583]: Tuesday Matins, Second Canon to the Forerunner, Ode 9, Theotokion. The Theotokos as the animate Cherubic Throne bearing and nursing the Sustainer of all creation; interceding against earthquakes, plagues, and calamities.
[^584]: Tuesday Matins, Post-Ode 9 Rubrics. Prescribing the Megalynarion 'It is truly meet' (*Dostoyno yest'* / Axios Estin) with prostration, followed by the Little Ektenia, Exapostilarion, and the customary Psalms of Lauds (LXX Pss. 148–150).
[^585]: Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, Sticheron 1, Tone 1. Solemn eschatological admonition: reminding the soul of the coming age and the imminent exposure of hidden vices before the divine Judge.
[^586]: Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, Psalm Verse 1. Versification from LXX Psalm 89:14–16 ('We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced and were glad...').
[^587]: Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, Sticheron 2, Tone 1. Evocation of the Parable of the Laborers in the Vineyard (Matthew 20:1–16), pleading to receive the wage of the eleventh hour despite chronic negligence.
[^588]: Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, Psalm Verse 2. Versification from LXX Psalm 89:17 ('And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us...').
[^589]: Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, For the Martyrs, Troparion 3, Tone 1. Commemoration of the holy martyrs as valiant soldiers of the great Heavenly King, defying the decrees of earthly persecutors.
[^590]: Tuesday Matins, Aposticha, Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Tone 1. The Virgin Mary as the pure habitation of the uncreated Light, besought to enlighten the contrite soul on the Day of Judgment.
[^591]: Tuesday Matins Dismissal and First Hour Rubric. Specifying the closing psalmody ('It is good to give thanks unto the Lord', LXX Ps. 91:2), the Troparion, Litany, Dismissal, and the subsequent First Hour.
[^592]: Tuesday Beatitudes, Stanza 1, Tone 1 (physical leaf p65, printed fol. 63). Antithesis between Adam expelled from Paradise through the food of disobedience (Genesis 3) and the Wise Thief entering Paradise through the Cross of Christ (Luke 23:42–43).
[^593]: Tuesday Beatitudes, Stanza 2, Tone 1. Penitential cry for daily conversion, beseeching the Lover of mankind to snatch the soul from torment.
[^594]: Tuesday Beatitudes, Stanza 3, Tone 1. Commemoration of St. John the Baptist as the forerunner of the unwaning Light Who shone forth from the radiant cloud of the Virgin.
[^595]: Tuesday Beatitudes, For the Martyrs, Stanza 4, Tone 1. The martyrs inheriting celestial rewards after enduring multifaceted physical tortures.
[^596]: Tuesday Beatitudes, Glory... Triadikon, Tone 1. Trinitarian doxology confessing one undivided Godhead in three distinct Hypostases.
[^597]: Tuesday Beatitudes, Both now... Theotokion, Tone 1. Dogma of Mary's perpetual virginity (*aeiparthenia*): remaining a pure virgin both before and after childbirth.
[^598]: Primary Day Opening: Wednesday, dedicated to the Precious and Life-Giving Cross of the Lord. Physical leaf p65 (printed fol. 63 = physical page 65). In the Byzantine and Ruthenian liturgical cycle, Wednesday commemorates the betrayal and Passion of Christ.
[^599]: Tuesday Evening (Vespers for Wednesday), Stichera of the Cross on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 1, Tone 1. Melodic Model (Podoben: 'O all-lauded martyrs' / Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченици). Christ nailed to the Cross as man to deify human nature and slay the venomous serpent, redeeming mankind from the curse (Galatians 3:13).
[^600]: Tuesday Evening, Stichera of the Cross on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 2, Tone 1. The voluntary self-emptying (*kenosis*) of Christ (Philippians 2:7–8), submitting to ignominious death upon the wood to restore immortality and pristine life to humanity.
[^601]: Tuesday Evening, Stichera of the Cross on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 3, Tone 1 (physical leaf p66, printed fol. 64). Invocation of the all-honorable Cross as the fortress of Christian rulers and the unifier of all the ends of the earth.
[^602]: Tuesday Evening, Stichera to the Theotokos on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 1, Tone 1. Melodic Model (Podoben: 'Joy of the heavenly ranks' / Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ). Stavrotheotokion: the sorrowful lamentation of the Mother of God standing beside the Cross of her Son (John 19:25–27).
[^603]: Tuesday Evening, Stichera to the Theotokos on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 2, Tone 1. Maternal grief of the unwedded Mother wounded by the sight of her Only-begotten Son dying upon the tree.
[^604]: Tuesday Evening, Stichera to the Theotokos on 'Lord, I have cried', Sticheron 3, Tone 1. Patristic reflection on the Virgin's labor: having brought forth Christ without birth-pangs in Bethlehem, she now experiences the piercing grief of maternal sorrow at Golgotha (Luke 2:35). The stanza spans leaves p66–p67.
[^605]: Tuesday Evening, Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion on 'Lord, I have cried', Tone 1. Sobbing lamentation of the Virgin beholding the pierced side and outstretched arms of the Savior on the Cross (John 19:34).
[^606]: Tuesday Evening Vespers Rubric. Order of service: evening hymn 'O Gladsome Light' (*Phos Hilaron*), the daily Prokeimenon, the prayer 'Vouchsafe, O Lord' (*Kataxioson*), and the Litany of Supplication.
[^607]: Tuesday Evening, Aposticha, Stichera of the Cross, Sticheron 1, Tone 1. The planting of the Cross on Calvary (Golgotha) as the flourishing of immortality flowing from Christ's pierced side.
[^608]: Tuesday Evening, Aposticha, Psalm Verse 1. Versification from LXX Psalm 122:1–2 ('Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes...').
[^609]: Tuesday Evening, Aposticha, Stichera of the Cross, Sticheron 2, Tone 1. The precious Cross hailed as an unbreakable rampart (*nerazrushena stina*) defending the faithful.
[^610]: Tuesday Evening, Aposticha, Psalm Verse 2. Versification from LXX Psalm 122:3–4 ('Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us...').
[^611]: Tuesday Evening, Aposticha, For the Martyrs, Troparion 3, Tone 1 (physical leaf p67, printed fol. 65). The spiritual trade or merchandise (*dobraya kuplya* / καλὴ πραγματεία) of the martyrs: surrendering temporal blood and mortal existence to purchase eternal heavenly glory.
[^612]: Tuesday Evening, Aposticha, Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion, Tone 1. Melodic Model (Podoben: 'Joy of the heavenly ranks' / Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ). The weeping Virgin contemplating the paradox of the Creator, Who holds creation in His fist (Isaiah 40:12), nailed bodily to the Cross.
[^613]: Tuesday Evening Dismissal Rubrics. Nunc Dimittis ('Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart', Luke 2:29–32), Trisagion Prayers, the Troparion of the Saint, and the liturgical Dismissal.
[^614]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Heirmos, Tone 1. The Song of Moses (Exodus 15:6: 'Thy right hand, O Lord, is become glorious in power...').
[^615]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Rubrical Refrain: 'Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us' (Прест҃а́ѧ Гг҃жѐ Бцⷣе, спаси́ на́съ).
[^616]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 1. The Incarnation as the renewal of fallen human nature: the incomprehensible Word taking flesh from the pure flesh of Mary to restore what was damaged by the ancestral transgression.
[^617]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 2. The Mother of the Divine Word honored as the ornament of earthly creation and the advocate of penitent sinners.
[^618]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Glory... Troparion 3. Marian spatial paradox (*Platytera ton ouranon*): the Virgin's womb shown to be more spacious than the heavens by containing the uncontainable Creator.
[^619]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 1, Both now... Theotokion. Petition for deliverance from the storm of worldly sorrows and the arrogance of spiritual foes.
[^620]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Heirmos. Christ knowing the infirmity of human nature and taking form therein, besought to gird the soul with heavenly power.
[^621]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 1 (physical leaf p68, printed fol. 66). Patristic antinomy of the Incarnation: the Plenitude (*pleroma*) emptied, the Unoriginate begun, the Invisible made visible, and the Nourisher of creation suckled at a human breast.
[^622]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Troparion 2. The Virgin Mary giving birth to the divine Physician and Lord Who opens the pathway to eternal life.
[^623]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Glory... Troparion 3. Deification (*theosis*) of human nature accomplished through the virginal childbearing of the All-immaculate Mother.
[^624]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 3, Both now... Theotokion. Contrite confession of a life squandered in spiritual indolence, praying for enlightenment through the light of repentance.
[^625]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Heirmos. Prophecy of Habakkuk 3:3 (the 'mountain overshadowed by the grace of God', prefiguring the Holy One of Israel proceeding from Mary).
[^626]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 1. The Theotokos as the divine palace of the Word, wherein He dwelt bodily to reconstruct fallen humanity.
[^627]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 2. The Divine Word entering Mary's womb to drive away the primordial curse and deify mankind through His unconfused hypostatic union.
[^628]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Glory... Troparion 3. Reversal of the Fall: Adam, who incurred death by tasting the forbidden fruit (Genesis 3:6), is revitalized and restored to Paradise through the Virgin's birthgiving.
[^629]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 4, Both now... Theotokion. Christ the Fountain of immortality stemming the torrent of mortal decay; prayer for the healing of spiritual passions.
[^630]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Heirmos (physical leaf p69, printed fol. 67). Allusion to Isaiah 26:9 and 60:1–3, praising Christ Who illuminated the world through His advent and Cross.
[^631]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 1. Christ proceeding from the Virgin as the unsetting Sun of Righteousness (Malachi 4:2), scattering the darkness of demonic ignorance.
[^632]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 2. Archangelic salutation (*Chairetismos*), honoring Mary for giving birth in the flesh to the Angel of Great Counsel (Isaiah 9:6 LXX).
[^633]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Glory... Troparion 3. Liberation from the tyrannical dominion of Satan accomplished through the Lord's incarnation in the virginal womb.
[^634]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 5, Both now... Theotokion. Christ clothing Himself in fallen humanity to deify it, with petition to strip the sinner of every vice.
[^635]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Heirmos. The Prayer of Jonah (Jonah 2:2–7) and Psalm 43:23 LXX ('We are accounted as sheep for the slaughter').
[^636]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 1. The seedless conception and miraculous virginity of Mary intact after giving birth (*virginitas in partu* and *post partum*).
[^637]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Troparion 2. Typological synthesis of Old Testament Marian types: Jacob's Ladder (Genesis 28:12), the overshadowed mountain (Habakkuk 3:3), the radiant cloud (Exodus 13:21), and the closed gate through which only the Lord enters (Ezekiel 44:1–3).
[^638]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Glory... Troparion 3. The miraculous birth of the hypostatic Word transcending the biological laws of nature to heal human brokenness.
[^639]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 6, Both now... Theotokion. Personal prayer presenting a soul infirm with transgressions to the Mother of God, seeking intercessory healing.
[^640]: Tuesday Compline, Sessional Hymn after Ode 6, Tone 1. Fortyfold repetition of 'Lord, have mercy' (*Kyrie eleison*) followed by Doxology and Sessional Hymn pleading protection through the Cross of Christ. The text concludes across leaves p69–p70.
[^641]: Tuesday Compline, Sessional Hymn conclusion (physical leaf p70, printed fol. 68). Solemn magnification of the Mother of God as the duty of the pious.
[^642]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Heirmos. The Song of the Three Holy Children in Babylon (Daniel 3:24–50), where the furnace becomes a mystical figure of the Virgin's womb sheltering the uncreated fire of the Godhead.
[^643]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 1. Legal shadows and prophetic enigmas fulfilled in the seedless virgin birth of Christ.
[^644]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 2. The Theotokos bringing forth the Dayspring from on high (*Anatole*, Luke 1:78) to illumine the faithful and disperse the dark night of impiety.
[^645]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Glory... Troparion 3. Typology of the Unburnt Bush on Mount Horeb (Exodus 3:2), prefiguring the womb of the Virgin Mary unharmed by the consuming fire of the Godhead (Hebrews 12:29).
[^646]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 7, Both now... Theotokion. Contrite supplication to the Mother of God as the haven of sinners, asking forgiveness of transgressions. Spans leaves p70–p71.
[^647]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Heirmos. Canticle of the Three Holy Children (Daniel 3:57–88 / Benedicite): the Hebrew youths shining in the Babylonian furnace purer than gold refined in a crucible.
[^648]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 1. Old Testament types and shadows realized in the historical reality of the Mother of the Lord.
[^649]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Troparion 2. The Divine Word adorned with unoriginate beauty, illuminating the hearts of believers.
[^650]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Glory... Troparion 3. Prophetic foretelling of the Mother of God, uniting the Church in the universal doxology of Daniel 3:57.
[^651]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Both now... Theotokion. Poetic apostrophe to the beauty of the Virgin Mary, who gave birth to the One Who is all-fair in divine beauty (Psalm 44:3 LXX). The stanza continues and concludes on physical leaf p71 (printed fol. 69).
[^652]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 8, Both now... Theotokion conclusion (physical leaf p71, printed fol. 69). Conclusion of the canticle praising the supernatural incarnation of Christ beyond natural causation.
[^653]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Heirmos. Typology of the Unburnt Bush on Mount Horeb (Exodus 3:2), invoked as an archetype of the virginal childbearing and protection against burning trials.
[^654]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 1. Human nature regaining pristine beauty and incorruptibility (*aphtharsia*) through the Incarnation of the Word.
[^655]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Troparion 2. The paradox of the Virgin nursing the Sustainer of all creation (Luke 11:27), addressing Mary as the mystical Ark of the Covenant.
[^656]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Glory... Troparion 3. Ascetic prayer for enlightenment against the darkening passions of spiritual sloth (*akēdia*).
[^657]: Tuesday Compline, Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Ode 9, Both now... Theotokion. Contrite expectation of the Dread Judgment and pleading the maternal advocacy of the Theotokos.
[^658]: Tuesday Compline Dismissal Rubric. Ordering the Megalynarion 'It is truly meet' (*Dostoyno yest'*) with a prostration, the Trisagion Prayers, Apolytikia, and standard Compline Dismissal.
[^659]: Wednesday Matins, Sessional Hymn 1 of the Cross, Tone 1. Christ's voluntary Crucifixion vanquishing demonic tyranny directly without angelic intermediary (Isaiah 63:9 LXX).

The 1895 Lviv Stauropegion edition preserves the official Galician Greek Catholic Austro-Hungarian imperial supplication: 'бл҃говѣ́рномꙋ їмпера́торꙋ на́шемꙋ' (our right-believing Emperor, referring to Franz Joseph I).

[^711]: Wednesday Matins, Second Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 4, Troparion 3. Spiritual despondency (*akēdia*) depicted as a tomb and chains of iron; pleading the vigilant awakening of Mary's prayer.
[^712]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 5, Heirmos, Tone 1. Allusion to Isaiah 26:9 and Philippians 4:7 ('the peace of God which surpasses all understanding').
[^713]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 5, Troparion 1. Cosmic convulsions at the Crucifixion: the eclipse of the sun, the splitting of rocks, and earthquakes (Matthew 27:51).
[^714]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 5, Troparion 2. Theological wordplay on 'rib' (Eve formed from Adam's rib in Genesis 2:21–22) and Christ's pierced rib (John 19:34) healing Adam's primordial transgression. Text spans columns on leaf p76.
[^715]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 5, For the Martyrs, Troparion 3. The chorus of martyrs as an incorruptible Paradise centered on Christ the true Tree of Life (Genesis 2:9; Revelation 22:2).
[^716]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 5, For the Martyrs, Troparion 4. Martyrdom drying up the sea of pagan polytheism while pouring out miracles of healing.
[^717]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 5, Stavrotheotokion. Patristic doctrine of Mary's virgin birth without maternal pangs, which she endures instead at the foot of the Cross (Symeon's prophecy, Luke 2:35).
[^718]: Wednesday Matins, Second Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Heirmos, Tone 1. Christ illuminating the ends of the earth by His Incarnation and Cross. Text spans leaves p76–p77.
[^719]: Wednesday Matins, Second Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Heirmos completion (physical leaf p77, printed fol. 75). Prayer for the divine light of the knowledge of God (*theognōsia*).
[^720]: Wednesday Matins, Second Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 1. The Mother of God as the restoration of Adam and Eve (*anaplasis*), besought to restore the ruined temple of the soul. Chanted twice.
[^721]: Wednesday Matins, Second Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 2. LXX Psalm 68:3 ('I am stuck fast in the mire of the deep') and the haven of salvation.
[^722]: Wednesday Matins, Second Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 5, Troparion 3. Prayer against the grip of chronic depression and despondency (*akēdia* / ꙋ҆ны́нїе).
[^723]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 6, Heirmos, Tone 1. The Prayer of Jonah (Jonah 2:1–10), allegorized as deliverance from the sea-monster of passions.
[^724]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 6, Troparion 1. Moses stretching out his hands during the battle against Amalek (Exodus 17:11–12) as a type of Christ's hands outstretched upon the Cross. Text spans columns on leaf p77.
[^725]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 6, Troparion 2. The blood and water from Christ's pierced side (John 19:34) and dogmatic confession of the Sixth Ecumenical Council (Constantinople III, 680–681) confessing Christ praised in two wills (divine and human, 'въ двою̀ во́лею').
[^726]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 6, For the Martyrs, Troparion 3. Allusion to LXX Psalm 115:6 ('Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints').
[^727]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 6, For the Martyrs, Troparion 4. The martyrs sustained in bodily dismemberment by the Incarnate Word Who assumed full human nature.
[^728]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 6, Stavrotheotokion. The Virgin lamenting the ingratitude of humanity towards the divine Physician. Text continues on physical leaf p78.

[^743]: Wednesday Matins, Second Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Troparion 2. Pauline theology of the New Adam (1 Corinthians 15:45–49) renewing fallen humanity grown old in corruption.
[^744]: Wednesday Matins, Second Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 7, Theotokion. Defense against demonic assaults and predatory spirits seeking the perdition of the soul.
[^745]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 8, Heirmos, Tone 1. The dew-producing furnace of Babylon (Daniel 3:49–50) prefiguring the miraculous, painless, and virginal conception and birth of Christ. Text spans columns on leaf p79.
[^746]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 8, Troparion 1. The voluntary Crucifixion of the Creator Who spanned the heavens (Isaiah 40:22) to undo Adam's fall.
[^747]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 8, Troparion 2. The hardening of heart of Christ's accusers contrasted with the quaking of inanimate creation (Matthew 27:51).
[^748]: Wednesday Matins, Canon to the Cross, Ode 8, For the Martyrs, Troparion 3. The historical tortures of Christian martyrs: stripped naked in the arena and having salt rubbed into fresh wounds, woven into a robe of salvation.
